# Evidence pack for RETROSPECTIVE.md

This file is the evidence behind `docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md`. The retrospective cites recommendations by the V-numbers used below (V1 to V88, the recommendations that survived their skeptics, in the order listed here) and by G-numbers (the completeness review's gap findings, at the end). Every recommendation was produced by one of twenty analysis lenses over this repository at `08dcaaa` and then faced three independent skeptics (evidence, counterfactual, cost); the skeptics' corrections are quoted under each item and are applied wherever the retrospective uses it. Refuted recommendations are listed after the verified ones and are never presented as recommendations. Text fields are condensed here; the untruncated verdicts run to about a megabyte and were not committed.

## Lens summaries

### scope-trajectory
Patterns began on 2026-08-29 as a ~9.7K-line portable test-pattern generator with an explicit out-of-scope list (PLAN §7), and changed kind four times in three weeks: on day 2 it became a playout switcher (looks, cues on F-keys, PGM/PVW CUT/TAKE, NDI receive, capture, web pages, streaming — ten commits, all breaking §7), on 09-03 a show-control desk (Run mode phases 1-6 from a separately supplied design), on 09-12 a multi-machine system (twin, endpoints, camera calibration), and on 09-13 a nodes/games/audience-phones platform (11,180 lines in one day from a single "consider mini games like Pong" idea). The CHANGELOG's framing of "one request from the field" per round is only partly true: rounds 9-28 answered the maintainer's own lists and quotes, rounds 29-30 and 39-53 were self-generated reviews and hardening, and rounds 56, 58, 65, 72, 75 and 78 answered written critique documents handed in; the only web "field research" (round 12) was indexed forum excerpts because the proxy refused the pages. Real-machine evidence existed from round 14/17 (crash exit codes, a rig-day crash) but as prose; the first desk files (Super Check) arrived at round 71 (09-16) and full logs at rounds 76-78 (09-17/18), and each time they found defects invisible to 2,400 headless tests: eleven TAKEs journaled Done with the outputs off, a 200-400 ms COM enumeration on the UI thread every second, a screencast refused at every rung, the show lock muting the desk's own browser, a phantom SCREEN UNPLUGGED at every boot. Nothing has ever been qualified on hardware: 21 QUALIFICATION matrices with zero filled rows, 19 checklists with zero ticks, and DRILL.md's own statement that the redundancy claims are about a simulated room; by a filename classification roughly 35-41% of the 139K source lines (twin, nodes, arcade, audience, calibration, NDI, capture, WebView2, WASAPI, EDID/display, PJLink, encoder, MIDI, OSC) sit behind those unqualified paths. The breadth tax is measurable: a late feature touches 38-63 files across ~15 hand-maintained surfaces (model, ShowAction/ActionSpec, a 1,443-line hand-written wire parser, OSC map, Companion JS module plus fixtures, help string constants, Eye, menus, cue sheet, assistant brief, XAML, REMOTE/COMPANION docs, then a 230-590-line papers commit), and a wire serializer to round-trip the vocabulary was only written at round 74. What demonstrably worked and should be kept is the one action vocabulary with journaled receipts and the "attempts are not facts" doctrine, the package-free Core with headless tests, and the practice of written critiques checked line by line — the round-78 field fault was pinned to the second from the journal and fixed with a regression test in one round. With hindsight and under the same constraints, the better plan was: close the show-laptop evidence loop at round 10 when Super Check existed, defer twin/nodes/arcade/audience/calibration until one machine was qualified, generate the surfaces from the ActionSpec table at round 16 instead of hand-threading every verb, switch on analyzers and the assembly split on day 1, and keep the papers in a shape (CHANGELOG + per-round notes + ADRs) that does not require rewriting a 10,386-line PLAN and a 1,700-line README ledger every round.

### architecture
The architecture lens finds a codebase whose central shapes were fixed in the first two days and never revised, only fenced. The show is 69 observable POCOs in one 2,952-line file (ShowState.cs), copied by JSON round-trip: every publish was a whole-show serialisation (twice under EDIT SAFE) until commit 3aabd81 on 2026-09-12, a GO serialised the show three times on the UI thread until round 56, and today looks, fingerprints, the cue validator, the recovery record, SamePicture and EDIT SAFE itself still clone or serialise through JsonUtil (42 Clone sites, 61 JsonSerializer calls). The edited show and the on-air programme are the same type, distinguished only by `AirState => Sandbox.ProgramState ?? State` (AppServices.cs:885), and that ambiguity is the proven root of the round-72 audio-follow bug, the round-72.4 stale-programme bug and the round-78.1 "eleven TAKEs" found on the first real rig. The composition root AppServices grew from 176 lines on day 1 to 2,459 (53 `new` in a 335-line constructor, 9 host interfaces, 41 files coupled to the concrete type against 6 to ServiceKernel), and the kernel/capability shape that ADR-011/012 later describe as the design arrived only at round 39 (677d554, day 15) as hardening item H4. MainViewModel reached 6,432 lines in seven days and was split verbatim into partials the same day (a308218); it is now one class in 23 files that still implements six page interfaces while the real page view models take `MainViewModel desk` as a dependency; ShowActions (185 switch arms in 20 files) and TwinService (round 51) followed the same split-by-line-range pattern. The wire has one shared enum (ShowActionKind, 220 members, round 16), which is the best structural decision in the repo, but the spelling of every verb is hand-maintained in at least nine places (230 `case "` literals in ControlProtocol, which reads ActionSpec zero times; a 245-row test table; a 326-line hand-built STATE JSON; 86 OSC rows; a 729-line Companion actions.js; 65 menu wire strings; unparsed help wire strings; a 109KB REMOTE.md with no generator and no test), which explains 67 parser commits, 28 commits that touched parser+REMOTE.md+Help together, the five fail-open toggles of round 72 and the "one version number in four files" note. The round-59 assembly split was cheap (nine commits in one day) and found nine misplaced rules, but Core still carries ~7,000 lines of desk vocabulary and prose (DeskMenus, Help/HelpBodies, Eye, SuperCheck, Walkthroughs), so "pure core" means "the app minus the toolkit". Process-wide statics (313 static classes, 92 static mutable collections) combined with the 582-site boot-the-whole-desk test strategy produced the round-62 host kill at 13.5 GB (root: the static FrameBudgets registry) and the round-64 census dig, which cost two rounds and a permanent CI lane. What demonstrably worked and should be kept: the headless whole-desk tests, the one executor with an origin, the one action vocabulary, and rules enforced as tests (ModuleRulesTests, ArchitectureFenceTests, CompanionModuleContractTests) — they are what made every late split a one-day operation with both suites green.

### testing
The suite is large (2,429 tests, ~87K test lines against ~139K source lines) and its centre of gravity is 661 [AvaloniaFact] tests that each boot the real desk headless with real Skia through tests/Patterns.App.Tests/TestApp.cs, serially, in one process; PLAN §5 planned only Core pixel/math/interop tests and never this desk suite, which grew by round-shaped test files (144 of 433 test files carry a "Round N" header; 38 test files were added on 09-05 alone). The suite is strong at scenario tests that assert published state and at count-based fences (publish counts, catalogue reads, the round-64 lifecycle census, module and architecture ratchets), and it demonstrably found real defects. What it structurally could not see was everything that lives only on Windows, only under a non-invariant culture, only under a timing budget, or only in a precondition no round's story mentioned: the 12-day per-second WASAPI enumeration in the tick (c59d659 on 09-04 to 8794153 on 09-16) was invisible because CaptureDevices() is "Empty off Windows" and no test bounded the tick's cost; the round-78 TAKE fault (introduced 82134a3 on 09-16) was invisible because none of six take test files ever set the outputs off or pressed TAKE twice on the same tile; the round-60 menus regression passed because DeskMenuAppTests asserted the menu model rather than driving the pointer; 145 culture sites and 39 undisposed fields waited for analyzers that Directory.Build.props could have carried from day 1. CI had a Windows runner from 08-29 (292029b) that only published an exe until round 64 (1c895ee, 09-15), when the first Windows test run immediately found newline and test-premise faults; even at HEAD only 24 of 661 App tests run on Windows. The App test host died three times at ~500 tests near 13.5 GB in round 62 while CI stayed green, forcing a memlog/census retrofit and the discovery that "forty-odd test files" never closed their desk. Hardware qualification was written, never run: docs/QUALIFICATION.md has 21 matrices and zero filled rows; SOAK.md and DRILL.md open with "nothing below is a claim"; rig days at rounds 17 and 24 yielded UI notes only, and the first real files from a show laptop arrived at rounds 76-78 and produced six field findings in two days. Doc-sync tests split into good contract tests (Companion lines.txt against ControlProtocol.Parse, module version, XAML hint-to-button, help-to-page) and one prose test (ChangelogTests) that exists because the ledger docs outgrew hand maintenance. With hindsight under the same constraints, the highest-value changes were cheap: run the tests on the Windows runner that already existed, turn the "attempts are not facts" doctrine into a suite-wide invariant over preconditions, turn on analyzers and a de-DE run in the day-1 props file, and replace empty qualification matrices with a fixed weekly laptop-and-TV script whose four log files feed each round's review.

### process-docs
Process lens: the repository was built as 78 "rounds" in three weeks, each closed by "the papers" — docs/PLAN.md (10,386 lines, 1MB, sections numbered in insertion order 1-8, 39, 40, 37, 38, 35...), docs/REVIEW.md, README.md's 1,680-line newest-first ledger and, from round 61, CHANGELOG.md — and those papers are also the AI agent's only working memory: there is no CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, glossary or docs index. PLAN.md claims at line 7 to be "written before implementation", but history shows the round sections are post-hoc narratives (round 65 touched PLAN only in its 13th, "papers" commit; EYE.md landed in the same commit as EyeLayout.cs and EyeTests.cs; §16 cites "commit 3"; 40 "**Proof" and 7 "Found on the way" markers), and by round 78 REVIEW.md has converged into the same "Done / Found on the way" form. Commit and branch discipline was absent by design (364 commits on one branch, subjects averaging 199 chars, max 2,873; 178 of 364 don't name their round; rounds 73 and 74 interleave), and the bill came at round 61: a 63-row retro tag table, a CHANGELOG back-filled by "reading the early history into rounds", and a belief that history began at round 15 which is false in this clone (root 227f628 on 2026-08-29, 92 commits before 09-06) yet is hard-coded in ChangelogTests.cs and tag-rounds.sh. Doc-to-code volume is modest overall (23,160 doc lines vs 278,932 code lines added over rounds 15-78, about 8%), but it is concentrated in the wrong artefacts: the two documents a newcomer would open first (README architecture, PLAN) are stale or unnavigable ("nine partial files" vs 23 on disk; tags "round-15…round-61" vs a table to 77), while the artefacts that work — ADR.md, MODULES.md, QUALIFICATION.md, ChangelogTests — arrived at rounds 59-65. Help prose lives as 61 C# string constants (94+75 commits) and the 109KB wire reference is hand-written and never test-checked although an ActionSpec table exists. The house vocabulary is consistent for operator terms but overloads "fence", "lane" and "seam" across unrelated mechanisms with no glossary. The hindsight plan under the same constraints is: an agent memory file plus per-round append-only notes from day 1, ADRs and a CHANGELOG from round 1, tags at every round close, short subjects with bodies, a glossary at round 9, help as data and the wire reference generated and fenced by a test — keeping the doc-as-contract tests, the QUALIFICATION honesty rule and the claim-by-claim critique method that demonstrably worked.

### operator-ux-surface
The operator surface grew by accretion, not from a design: the only design artefact ever named is the round-8 "Patterns Run Mode" review (PLAN §8, not in the repo), which produced the five-group rail, the 18-page table pinned by a test and the Run surface; everything before (10 tabs on day 1, 14 by 30 Aug, 17 by 3 Sep) and after (29 pages by round 66, 12 of them on BUILD) was added one page per feature. The closest things to a concept model or a naming table are code tables that arrived late — Shell.Pages at round 8, DeskPages in Core at round 74 — and they cover page headers only, never the nouns or the settings, so of 165 candidate operator nouns 156 appear in the help and 143 appear in both the help and the wire reference, one word ("group") carried two meanings until the maintainer said so at round 17, and page names inside prose went stale within days ("Admin → Switcher" written 5 Sep for a page renamed Machine on 3 Sep, "the Outputs page" written into right-click menus at round 67 for a header that vanished at round 8, README still saying eighteen pages and four SHOW CONTROLS rows against 29 and 7). The help is 51.7K words of hand-written C# string constants edited across 102 commits; the Switcher body is 1,091 words in 23 sentences averaging 47 words with a 149-word sentence, and 16 bodies contain a sentence of 100+ words, so an operator cannot learn the wall from it. The "instant-UX pass" (§14.3) was a performance pass; the UX changes that did happen (§20.7-20.9 round 15, §28.4-28.5 round 19, §80 round 62, §91 round 73) were reactions to the maintainer's own reports — "hard to find", "screens of settings the operator scrolled past", menus that opened nothing for two rounds — and the first real-laptop evidence at round 78 found an operator pressing TAKE eleven times in 27 seconds with the outputs off while the desk said Done. What the UI got right is the vision-mixer model itself: PGM/PVW panes and a wall of true miniatures with a tally, EDIT SAFE on by default, a Run surface with one gate, the take-plan line, refusals with reasons, a page table pinned by a test, a laptop fit test that has held through 29 pages, LazyPage, and ? TIPS; the round-78 fix stayed inside that model rather than replacing it. A day-1 plan under the same constraints would have been a dozen-noun concept table in Core that every label, help topic, menu and wire verb resolves through, a page budget with a one-place-per-noun rule, help skeletons generated from the action and page registries with a sentence-length lint, and a recorded 15-minute operator script on the real laptop every five rounds; its absence cost the 1,413-line DeskMenus, the 51.7K-word help, five desk surfaces for one screen, the round-73 operator's list and the round-78 field confusion.

### assistant-llm
The LLM assistant arrived in one 2,460-line commit at round 13 (7ad2166, 2026-09-05) on the maintainer's own round-13 list, not on any operator request: FIELD-RESEARCH.md (the round-12 survey of 14 operator problem areas) never mentions an assistant or AI, and neither do the field rounds 76-78 whose evidence came from a real show laptop. It is a "fenced model that drafts proposals the desk applies": no tools, a regex gate, a prose fence, a closed all-required JSON schema, a lenient parser, and an Apply step that builds looks, cues, screens and designs through the same Core paths the desk uses; that half is well made and had no assistant-caused show incident in 65 rounds. The brief the model reads, however, is a hand-maintained prose serialiser (ShowBrief.Summarise, Assistant.cs:492-718) fed by a 30-member ShowFacts that the desk gathers from eleven services on the UI thread on every ask, and every later desk-state feature (health round 53, devices round 31, signals/machine/commissioning round 65, the God's Eye round 66, residency round 69, audio routing round 72) added a section to it, with no size or token bound, no prompt-cache breakpoint, no latency or usage read, and no test against a single real reply. The only contact with the live service recorded in the repository is the round-17 400 error "The compiled grammar is too large" (PLAN §24.2), fixed blind because the build box had no key; the manual checklist rows that would have exercised it were never recorded as run, and the tool-runner and pre-show-check work planned at round 31 (§48.5) never happened. Round 37 put the assistant on the wire and routed audience-wall moderation through the operator's own conversation object (shared Busy flag, full brief per audience message, audience text appended to the conversation history), reversing round 13's "never on the wire" rule. The model id is a compile-time constant ("claude-opus-5", AssistantService.cs:33, unchanged since day one, SDK pinned at 12.46.0), a retired id would surface as a raw API message with no fallback, and the key sits in plaintext JSON on a USB-stick-portable install. With hindsight, at round 13 the assistant should have been a thin edge over the data the desk already emits (STATE, Super Check, the Eye's lines) with the prompt and model id as data, recorded-transport fixtures from the first real replies, and cost/latency on the status line; the proposal/apply fence and the cloud-not-local decision are the parts to keep.

### integrations-companion
The integrations really are a second codebase, and they were born together with the wire on day two (e43e28b, 2026-08-30: ControlProtocol.cs 135 lines, ControlService.cs 431, REMOTE.md 49, main.js 262, module 1.0.0). The Companion module went through 36 version bumps in 19 days (every package.json commit is a bump; 13 on 2026-09-05 alone, 6 on 2026-09-16; 15 releases across the four days of rounds 54–74) because it hand-mirrors the desk's vocabulary in 127 action definitions, 156 preset keys, hand-declared variables and feedbacks, and because Companion 5 refuses a re-import of the same version (discovered at round 57.1 after three rounds had shipped under 3.0.0). Round 54.1 (aebd42e) was a genuine rewrite (a 2,287-line main.js deleted, 4,681 lines added) forced by having started on module-base 1.x/node18 with apiVersion "0.0.0" when Companion 5 and base 2.x (March 2026) were already current. The wire itself is a hand-written grammar: ControlProtocol.Parse grew from 164 to 1,443 lines (230 case labels, 14 nested switches, 48 alias case lines, 104 top-level verb families of which roughly a quarter are aliases), mirrored by a second hand switch (WireWriter, 203 of 220 kinds, missing the AUDIO ROUTE/ROUTING/VOG, PLAN and COUNTDOWN FOLLOW families the module does send), a third (OscMap.ToLine, 86 reference rows vs 77 in REMOTE.md), 93 verb literals inside nine HTML pages embedded as C# string constants, 64 wire literals in DeskMenus, and a 109KB REMOTE.md touched in 97 commits with 60 per-round annotations inside its rows; no test compares any two of the five partial verb tables (Verbs 245 rows, REMOTE.md 269, lines.txt 296, OscMap 86, actions.js 127) and no doc is generated. Because there was never a protocol version (HELLO carries only module=x.y.z; the only version, DescriptorVersion 1 on NAV/MENU replies, arrived at round 75.4 and the module never reads it) every rename became a permanent alias (STOPALL as one token because an older build misparsed STOP ALL, SPOTIFY/MUSIC, TRACK/AUDIO, PAGE/WEB, PDF/SLIDES/DECK, LT/LOWERTHIRD, GO/STOP frozen to their day-two meanings) and the fail-open latch bug across six verb families survived until round 72.5 because ON/OFF/TOGGLE was re-typed per verb. Two decisions demonstrably worked and should be repeated: the round-16 one-vocabulary rule (every surface — TCP, HTTP, OSC, MIDI, devices, management server, menus — becomes a line and then a ShowAction, so the 72.5 fix was 24 parser lines applied to every surface at once), and keeping the module in the monorepo with cross-language contract tests and CI (lines.txt parsed by the desk's suite, palette parity read from palette.js, the fixture's keys checked against STATE, the version constant read from a .cs file by a JS test). The better plan under the same constraints was a versioned verb registry as data from day two, with the parser, writer, OSC map, Companion definitions, help and REMOTE.md generated from it and a protocol number in HELLO/STATE, and a module that is a generic client of a desk-served descriptor — which is exactly what the Navigator became at round 74.

### original-product-core
The day-1 test-pattern generator survives inside the round-78 desk almost untouched at the renderer level and almost unreachable at the operator level. The 14 day-1 renderers are essentially frozen (BarsPattern.cs is byte-identical apart from the round-59 move; only 30 of 364 commits touch the pattern folder; the 13 day-1 pixel asserts still pass through the real engine and were extended, not rewritten), four kinds were appended (Multiview, Fractal, Reactive, TestCard) and the 1:1 device-pixel transform is the same code as day 1 — but Focus and Geometry never got a pixel assert, 1:1 is fenced only inside the engine (no window-level test) and no real display has ever confirmed it (the only field rig is an LG TV at 1280x720@50 under a 60 fps show). What grew around the renderer is what damaged the product: the model went 193 to 2,952 lines (102 commits), the snapshot the engine takes now carries take/arm/freeze runtime, and the operator's path to a grid went from four steps and one latch (pick screens, pick pattern, F5 GO) to a 30-page, five-rail shell with three latches that must align — PREP/SHOW mode (PREP refuses OUTPUTS ON), EDIT SAFE on by default (a pattern chosen on the Pattern page changes only the preview until TAKE/CUT), and OUTPUTS ON on Shift+F5 (F5 became a look key on day 2) — none of which the README Quick start mentions. The field's eleven TAKE presses journaled Done with the outputs off (PLAN §96.1) is the direct product of those latches plus an ActionResult that has no observed-effect field; round 78 answered it with words on a Done result and a refusal for no-op takes, not with a status that says nothing reached a screen. Portability's feature-detection promise held (NDI, WebView2, libVLC, yt-dlp, Spotify and the Anthropic key all degrade with a note), but the exe became an 88 MB-compressed-then-uncompressed two-process launch whose start-up is measured in nine phases with amber at 8 s, fenced at 60 s in the suite, never timed on Windows per PLAN itself, and never recorded from the field; the field's numbers instead show the desk's own monitors starving the render clock to 12-16 Hz on an i5 laptop. Architecturally a CLI could render a pattern in about forty lines (RenderTestHarness proves the path) but it links the 50K-line show core, and no NodeKind (Desk, Caller, Arcade, Timer) can open an output, so the smallest process that puts a pattern on a wall is still the whole desk. The v1 product was never tagged, released or rig-qualified: tags start at round 15 (retroactively), Releases at round 61, QUALIFICATION.md has 0 of 21 rows filled, 19 checklists carry no run, and show control began on calendar day 2 (e43e28b). Under the same constraints, tagging and rig-day-qualifying v1 before round 3, keeping a single-state rig-day role beside the desk, and refusing to report Done for a picture no screen could show would have preserved the simple path and caught the field faults thirteen rounds earlier.

### platform-hygiene
The stack itself held up: Avalonia 11.3 + SkiaSharp gave a Windows-only show tool a headless test suite on Linux (661 AvaloniaFacts boot the whole desk), the .NET 8→10 move cost 21 lines, every package is pinned exactly and the native ones (LibVLCSharp 3.10.1, LibVLC 3.0.23.1) are at their latest stable. What the repository paid for was not the choice of libraries but the order in which the discipline around them arrived. Engineering hygiene that fits in one Directory.Build.props (analyzer fences, a banned-API list, one culture) was added at round 70 as a 234-file, +2,000/−521 retrofit after the disposal and fire-and-forget classes had already bitten at rounds 29, 30, 33, 62 and 64; the day-1 props explicitly set InvariantGlobalization=false and nothing about analysis. There is no threading ADR (ADR-011 is lifecycle and clocks), and the same UI-dispatcher seam was reworked at rounds 30, 33, 39, 45, 59, 64, 70 and 71, while synchronous work on the desk tick (round 29 disk listing, round 56 three serialisations per GO, round 71 a 200–400 ms COM enumeration every second) was a class no analyzer catches and the tick-budget row that would have shown it existed from round 14 but was first read from a real machine at round 71. The process model was argued well in PLAN §12/§14.2, but the child-process host built at round 16 (2,286 lines) isolated the encoder, the one native edge with no field fault on record, while the decoders — the leading candidate for the two 0xC0000005 exits in the round-14 watchdog log — stayed in-process to HEAD. WebView2 was hosted in a hand-made hidden Win32 window and fed through DevTools JPEG screencast, the one frame path in the field comparison that encodes and decodes every frame; three rounds (55, 68, 77) engineered it and the field log at round 78 shows it refused at every rung (E_INVALIDARG, WebFrameSource.cs:436), the page living on the 20 fps screenshot poll from round 12. CI had a Windows publish job from day 1 but no Windows tests until round 64, on a Hyper-V runner with a 1 Hz display, no GPU and no audio endpoint; 19 checklists, 21 qualification matrices, SOAK and DRILL were written and never recorded as run, though a laptop produced a 70-minute watchdog log at round 14 and each later ad-hoc read (55, 71, 76–78) found real platform faults. The release side is a genuine keep: the day-2 libVLC bundling failure led to a verified bundle, an exact-package manifest, --verify-runtime in CI and a rollback workflow with its own test job. The assistant's use of the Anthropic SDK is current (claude-opus-5, output_config.format, refusal handling), though the model id is a compile-time constant with no settings override.

### defect-archaeology
The defect record here is unusually complete: docs/REVIEW.md carries a "found on the way" list for every round from 29 to 78, and the earlier faults live in PLAN sections and commit bodies. From those sources I catalogued 183 distinct defects (pre-release review through round 78.4). By root cause the two largest classes are "attempts reported as facts" (33) and "two copies of the truth" (25), followed by lifetime/ownership (24), UI-thread/threading (21), UI-toolkit behaviour (12), protocol/security (11), persistence/multi-instance (10), native edges (9) and culture (3), with 35 one-off logic errors. Both leading classes were already visible on days 2-4 (the sandbox leaking to air in 65ffe9c/527b66a/9aabfe9/35b0945; a status-plus-words ActionResult in 0c2c219) and recurred to the last commit: the field's first real evidence at round 78 was eleven TAKEs journaled Done with the outputs off and a tile copying its own picture over itself, one instance of each class. Roughly a fifth of the catalogue (38 items) names the earlier round whose change introduced it, 26 of those crossing a round boundary, and the per-round tails show the pattern: three or four feature rounds of 1-2 commits each (31-33, 35-38, 54-55, 60, 65-69, 73-74) followed by a correction round (34, 39, 56-58, 62, 70-72, 75-78). Measured from the tag table with git diff --numstat, rounds 29-78 split into 22 correction-dominant rounds (29% of src lines added, 42% of test lines, 94 of 202 commits), 19 new-capability rounds (46% of src lines), 4 refactor and 5 mixed rounds. The sound-follows-picture rule was semantically rewritten in nine rounds (25, 26, 55, 58, 69, 72, 76, 77, 78) and the take/PVW rule in seven, both because a picture-derived reading was taken from the edited state or from an assumed rule rather than from what the room could see. Still open at HEAD: everything that needs the rig (0 of 21 qualification rows, DRILL never run), the WebView2 screencast E_INVALIDARG with no known cause, the 50 Hz display under a 60 fps show, the monitor-sinks scheduler, the policy registry key, and a handful of "measured and left" timing assumptions from rounds 30-58.

### state-domain
The state model is one 2,952-line observable ShowState (35 object sections plus four scalars, 52 [JsonIgnore] chrome properties inside it) that is at once the editable document, the settings file, the show file (*.patshow.json is the same JSON with a name), the source of the frozen programme (SandboxService.Enter JSON-clones it), the payload of the recovery record (RecoverySnapshot carries a whole ShowState? Air) and the thing snapshots are composed from; JSON serialisation is the clone, the equality test (SamePicture), the identity hash (TransitionKeyFor) and the persistence format throughout, and eighteen sidecar file kinds each grew their own store and atomic-write code until a single persistence lane was retrofitted at rounds 56 and 65. The action layer was not a day-1 design: the day-1 PLAN has no action, cue or journal; day 2's CommandRouter drove MainViewModel commands directly; ShowActions.Execute arrived at commit 26 (Phase 1, 09-03) with 30 kinds, Phase 3 the same day added a second vocabulary (CueActionKind, 27 members, a 65-row map), and round 16 (commits 114-115, 09-06) deleted both maps at a cost of about 61 files and 1.7K/1.5K lines, after which every verb is one enum member, one executor case and one ActionSpec row (220 kinds today). What an action returns is still ActionResult(Status, string Message): the journal writes Status.ToString() and words, and the field's eleven ScreenTake rows journaled Done with the outputs off (PLAN §96.1) because the result describes the plan, not an observed effect; the doctrine "attempts are not facts" was therefore re-applied piecemeal at rounds 43, 56, 64, 72, 75, 76 and 78, each adding a fragment (receipts, execution ids, tickets, shape keys, EffectOf, UnseenNote) that a typed effect on the result would have carried from the start. The surfaces that must agree are hand-written in parallel: ControlProtocol (1,443 lines, 262 Act() sites, 67 commits), WireWriter (a second hand inverse added at round 74), OscMap (675 lines), CommandRouter.StateJson (61 rows), Help/HelpBodies (75/94 commits), DeskMenus and five Companion JS files; AudioFollow touches 28 files and TAKE NEXT 19, and ActionSpec is read by the cue editor, validator, sheet and assistant but by none of those surfaces, so the replication is held by contract tests (a 116-line wire table, a 296-line Companion lines fixture, WireWriter round trips) instead of by generation. Identity was never designed: the screen id has been `{index}:{w}x{h}@{x},{y}` since day 1, so every hot-plug, mode change and re-index re-identifies the rig, which cost a day-2 fix, RenameScreen at 09-04, six memory fields plus HotPlugWatch at round 32 and black projectors at the venue at round 76.4; canvas keys derive from screen ids and must be rekeyed; the daily schedule and presenter steps still name looks by name while looks have ids since schema 4. The one-engine-many-sinks design held (one ShowSnapshot drawn by outputs, NDI, stream, preview and thumbnails; the encoder left the process exactly when PLAN §12 said it should), but its "immutable-ish" clone-per-publish pipeline cost 10.5 ms per publish on the UI thread until round 29's section Compose and MarkPublished latch, three whole-show serialisations per GO until round 56, and a coalescing step that round 29 declined because 361 test references read Bus.Current synchronously.

### peers-and-prior-art
Patterns knew its peers late and in the wrong order for its core models. Day 1 (PLAN §1-§7) names no product; the line protocol and a Companion module were built on day 2 (e43e28b) on a module base that Companion 5 could not load, the switcher's PGM/PVW/CUT/TAKE the same day (a204d41), and the cue stack on 09-03 (93314a1), all before the first peer study (PRO-FEATURES.md, round 11, 1989705, 09-05) and the first field research (FIELD-RESEARCH.md, round 12, web excerpts because the proxy refused forum pages). Where research preceded building (PJLink and endpoints at round 31, warp/blend at 32, audio matrix at 55, web video at 68, MIDI learn at 73) the designs held with few reworks; where building preceded research the repo paid in rounds: three hand-written control vocabularies merged at round 16 (1a0eb49, 73 files), the take rules re-derived at rounds 26, 60, 63, 67, 72, 75, 76 and 78 until the first real show log showed eleven TAKEs journaled Done with the outputs off, the Companion module ported to base 2.x at round 54 (+4,765/-2,694 lines) and its version rule found at 57, and "what is heard where" answered seven times between rounds 26 and 78. The cue model is a partial, unnamed mirror of QLab (list order as truth, Broken cues that GO refuses, auto-follow, a per-step relative wait added at round 24) with deliberate and well-argued divergences (standby-id fence, confirm, hold); QLab's pre/post-wait and continue modes were explicitly declined at round 72. PJLink and NDI were adopted faithfully and held; OSC was bolted on at round 11 as a 1:1 map onto the line protocol with no query replies beyond pong/status/error; Art-Net, sACN, MSC and LTC were named as gaps at rounds 11-12 and never built, and two of round 12's "small" items (a configurable minimum time between GOs, a deck next-page source) were never built either. The network check: WebSearch worked (QLab cue-sequence docs, Companion base v2 compatible with Companion >= 4.3, Event Master JSON-RPC arm/unarm destinations); WebFetch to qlab.app was refused by the egress proxy, so finer peer-model claims below are marked inferred. The strongest peer-facing practices to keep are the desk-to-module contract test, the PJLink-to-the-byte profile, and the evidence rule that finally made the take a transaction (ADR-015).

### release-ops
Through the release/versioning/rollback/operations lens, the repository shows a project that shipped a portable exe from CI on day 1 (PLAN §6, 292029b) but never gave a build an identity: Directory.Build.props has carried <Version>1.0.0</Version> since c584687 (2026-08-29) and no later commit changed it, and every operator-facing surface (the log banner, the Admin page's "Build:" line, the STATE wire, the Super Check row, the Eye, the mDNS beacon, the management check-in) reads Assembly.GetName().Version, so every build ever made says 1.0.0. Rounds became the real version scheme but only at round 61 (2026-09-15, three days before the end): CHANGELOG.md was back-filled for 60 rounds in 20 commits, tag-rounds.sh's table was hand-extended in every papers commit after that, the round boundaries before 39 were read from dates because a third of the commits did not name their round, the table starts at round 15 on a false belief that history begins 2026-09-06 (92 commits and rounds 9-14 are in git), the agent session could not push tags at all (403 five times), and the default branch sat at round 28, so the rollback button the round built was never reachable. The pipeline itself is sound where it counts (a release is gated by the Ubuntu tests and a Windows publish that runs the exe's own --verify-runtime, the libVLC payload is pinned to the restore's assets file since round 64, and CI found real defects four times) but disproportionate elsewhere: windows-smoke and the 100-cycle lifecycle job do not gate the release, there is no code signing, no NuGet lock, no nightly run, the local publish scripts and the CI job diverge (the scripts copy WebView2Loader.dll and createdump.exe beside the exe and the local full script still takes "the newest libVLC in the cache", which the CI job explicitly forbids), and the manifest the docs describe (Avalonia, SkiaSharp, a libVLC hash) was never written. The rollback workflow (282 lines of YAML and bash plus a CI job) re-commits an older tree for a user who could not press its button, while the one real rollback hazard, SettingsStore loading a show file newer than the exe with no guard and re-stamping it lower, is unaddressed, and README already names the sufficient answer: download the older Release. The update system built at round 12 speaks a third version vocabulary (patterns.update.json "1.2.0" versus a constant running "1.0.0" versus the manifest's round/commit) and nothing in the tree produces an update package. Operationally the field files, when they finally arrived at rounds 77-78, did answer the questions (the show log pinned the take fault to the second, patterns.log answered the screencast hypothesis, the Super Check exposed the 50 Hz TV), but the journal row had no observed-effect field, so eleven takes were "Done" with the outputs off, patterns.log and the show log run on different clocks, the support bundle omits the quality profile the diagnosis relied on, and the build the laptop ran is known only from the maintainer's word.

### portability-and-native-runtime
The portable-exe promise was cheap to state and expensive to keep honest. The exe is a self-contained single file with natives self-extracted (IncludeNativeLibrariesForSelfExtract=true, compression on at day 1 and turned off at 09-07, ReadyToRun since round 16), and the papers record 88 MB as a compressed figure with no current size or measured field boot time anywhere; the README still says "no registry" while the code has written HKCU GPU preferences since 08-31 and HKCU sound/notification keys since 09-12, one of them a policy key the field machine denied. Every native edge is feature-detected through a candidate-folder table and fails with words (NDI, libVLC, WebView2), and that discipline worked: NdiInterop has three commits in twenty days and no field failure. The costs concentrated on the two in-process frame producers: libVLC decode (two unexplained 0xC0000005 exits on the maintainer's laptop at round 14, a safe-run/createdump kit built blind, and the round 58/64/69 fence, pool, census and residency machinery, about +10.6K lines, whose libVLC lock/unlock path the review admits was never run) and WebView2 (screencast added 09-14, refused with E_INVALIDARG at every ask on the only field machine from the first day to the last, cause still unknown, no test ever creates a CoreWebView2). The repository built the right isolation mechanism once, at round 16 (a shared-memory ring, a line protocol, a job object, watchdog backoff) for the stream encoder, named the decoder as "next" in code and paper, and never built it; ADR-010 defers renderer isolation citing PLAN sections that do not contain a render-host design. A native fault today still ends the desk process: the room sees the desktop for the backoff plus the boot, covered only by a standby twin. A Windows runtime check arrived at round 64, seventeen days after the first native dependency, and it still does not probe WebView2, NDI or PDFium; the CI runtime reports themselves could not be fetched from here. With hindsight the better plan under the same constraints is a core exe with no natives, sidecar hosts for decode and browser capture on the ring the encoder already uses, a Windows lane that launches the built exe against every native from day 1, and a footprint receipt that makes "writes only beside itself" a tested claim rather than a README sentence.

### ai-collaboration-process
The record shows a maintainer who directed by five kinds of ask: numbered feature lists (rounds 9-13, 17, 19, 62, 63, 73), whole-of-system questions (12, 18, 31/32, 59, 69, 70), review requests (29, 39), critiques with roadmaps handed in as documents (34, 43, 45, 56, 58, 65, 69.7, 74, 77.6) and field reports, first as sentences (14, 15, 17, 55, 62) and then with the desk's own files (71, 76, 77, 78). Judged by what later rounds had to undo, the asks that paid best per round were the single-cause field report with files (round 71 removed a UI-thread COM enumeration that had been in the view-model layer since 2026-08-30/09-04 and through every review), the analyzer question (round 70 fixed 145+39+55+26+29 sites in one round after 69 rounds with no analyzer beyond TreatWarningsAsErrors) and the module/fence questions (59, 69) whose tests still stand; the longest tails came from the two big feature days (2026-09-03's six Run-mode phases created two action vocabularies the same day, fixed by a 73-file round 16 after the maintainer wrote "the problem is still there", plus a GO record serialised three times on the UI thread until round 56; 2026-09-05's +58K/-8K day was followed by rounds 14-16 of crash and refactor work, round 29's 16 findings and a view-model peel asked for four times). Self-review found real defects when it had an instrument (13 on day 1 including a mutex that never engaged, 16 at round 29, 8 leak roots by heap dump at 64, 13 found-on-the-way at 65) but consistently missed its own assumptions and anything only a real machine shows: round 60's menus opened nothing because the test showed the flyout by hand, the Linux footprint test stayed green while the Windows exe loaded both assemblies, and round 67's tile-take rule journaled eleven fades that never happened until the show log arrived at round 78. External critiques varied (round 45's two reviews were partly stale and wrong; round 69.7's was 13 for 13 real in code that had passed the agent's own 63.5 and 64 lists), and the agent's habit of checking every critique claim by claim at a named line, declining renames, containers and six-layer restructures with reasons, was one of the collaboration's genuine strengths. The agent's habits cost the record itself: prose commit subjects averaging 199 characters (max 2,873) with the real message duplicated in bodies, three author identities, 925 "round N" references in 201 of 535 source files, a 1 MB PLAN whose headings run 8, 39, 40, 37, 38, a README whose "What it does" is a 1,700-line ledger, and a CHANGELOG written from the plan rather than from git that misstates the history. The standing brief's growth from "stable, instant" (round 15) to "AI at each step, Companion always considered" (56) to "the wire and Companion considered at each step, the assistant told" (62) did drive breadth (one verb in 33 files, thirteen Companion module versions between rounds 54 and 74, seven readers per verb by the agent's own count) while the qualification record stayed empty: 0 of 21 matrix rows, 19 rig checklists with no result column, a drill record never filled, and a round-14 request to read the laptop's dump never answered. The day-1 agreement below is built from those costs: the rig as the gate, one-subject rounds, analyzers and fences from the first commit, every review treated as a hypothesis with a failing test, conventions for the record, and the parts of the agent's practice that demonstrably worked kept.

### persistence-compat
The show file itself is the best-built persistence in the repository: since day 1 (19e4944) SettingsStore has written atomically with a .bak, quarantined corrupt files, never let a file stop startup, and since 09-03 read unknown enum members tolerantly; eleven in-place schema steps (v2..v11, 2026-08-30 to 09-16) are version-gated, idempotent and reasoned in comments, and no incident in PLAN or REVIEW involves a lost show file. Everything around that file accreted instead: 18 named artefacts beside the exe plus five folders, twelve separate copies of the temp-then-move idiom in eleven files, and a "one persistence lane" (ADR-008) that arrived at round 56/65 and covers only settings.json, recovery.json and (partly) the playhead file while ownership, known-good, quality, credentials, crash and watchdog markers write directly. The recovery record is the churn hotspot: created on day 2 as a two-boolean sidecar, it grew to carry a whole ShowState by 09-07, and its lifecycle (who writes it, who clears it, when) was re-cut on 09-01, round 22 (four holes), round 43 (ownership record failing open), round 56 (three serialisations per GO), round 65.12, round 76 (Deliberate flag, playheads) and round 78 (a second desk clearing the first desk's record); it still has no version, its Air state is restored without Migrate, and its write reports success even when the write failed. Migrations are tested only synthetically: no fixture corpus exists (no JSON under tests/, no EmbeddedResource), most tests build `new ShowState { SchemaVersion = N }` from the current model, and one test hard-codes the current schema number and was edited on every bump. Compatibility runs one way: the round-61 build (schema 10) that the rollback workflow can put back opens a round-78 (schema 11) file without any check, stamps it 10 and would rewrite it on its first autosave without the properties added in rounds 62-78; the README's rollback section never mentions the show file, and nine of the sidecars appear in no operator document. The round-76 secret scrub is the only deliberately destructive load-time change (rows removed, operator told); the v10/v11 badge steps are non-destructive by construction; the claimed round-62 pointer-flag removal could not be verified in history. With hindsight, under the same constraints, the cheap missing pieces were a saved show file per schema bump as a test fixture, a forward-version rule and a documented file catalogue at the moment the rollback workflow was written, and the round-43 SidecarRead/SidecarWrite store type applied to every sidecar from the day the second one appeared.

### security-control-network
The control network had no threat model and no authentication for 65 of 78 rounds. PLAN §1-§8 never mentions trust, an attacker, tokens or a bind address (the only network sentence is about NDI, docs/PLAN.md:27-28); "threat model" appears nowhere in PLAN, REVIEW, ADR or README. The first remote listener (e43e28b, 2026-08-30) bound TCP 9697 and HTTP 9696 to IPAddress.Any with no credential and the README advertised it to "any phone/tablet on the network"; by round 10 (3fb739f) that open port ran 51 action kinds and 33 wire verbs, by round 30 121/153, by round 50 175/207 and by round 64 191/231, all still ungated, with the admin passcode riding in `?pass=` URLs until 65. The first security-shaped decision was round 12's AdminGate (8e0949c: constant-time compare, five tries then a 60 s lockout, a management token echoed in replies, SHA-256 on downloaded packages, https-or-private URL rule), and its own docs stated the position that held until round 65: "the passcode travels on the LAN in the clear ... a fence around a site" and "the folder is the trust boundary". The twin got there earlier than the control port (key mandatory at 34, HMAC-over-nonce at 44, with forged-join tests) and never needed a trust retrofit again, whereas the control port needed 65.5 (pairing token, bind, 30 files), 65.12 (Secrets one list), 75.1 (RECORD/NAV DECK outside the gate, passcode in the action feed and the journal since round 12) and 76.6 (passcode in ToString, MIDI bindings, old journals, bundle text). Every secret is plaintext JSON beside the exe (patterns.settings.json, patterns.assistant.json, patterns.spotify.json; zero uses of ProtectedData/DPAPI/TLS anywhere), and the twin mirrors device secrets over an authenticated but unencrypted link by default (SendSecrets = true). The update channel authenticates the transfer, not the publisher: a hand-dropped zip needs only a version and a zip-slip check, the management server's SHA-256 travels in the same reply as the URL, its reply is trusted on a plaintext token echo (string.Equals, http permitted on private ranges), and applyUpdate/restart bypass the passcode gate, so the management server is remote code execution by design. Today's defaults are still open on every interface (Control.Enabled = true, Token = "", Bind = "") and only the operator's NEW TOKEN closes them, while OSC, the twin listener and the beacon ignore both the bind address and the token, and the desk's own WebView2 content runs on the token-exempt loopback with only cue verbs behind a client header. The audience port (rounds 37-39) is the one surface built fail-closed from the start and is the model to repeat.

### distributed-twin-nodes
The multi-machine layer (twin, follower/caller and timer nodes, arcade, audience hub, rig-day gamification, room clock) was built almost entirely between 2026-09-12 and 2026-09-14 — rounds 31 to 52 — and accounts for about 12.6K surviving src lines (~9% of src), 8.3K test lines / 158 test methods, 32 of the 220 ShowActionKind verbs, and 27 commits worth +32.3K/-3.6K lines (10.6% of every insertion in the repository); twelve rounds were wholly this work and roughly fifteen round-equivalents of 78 in all. The first twin commit is 88c8c77 in round 31 (PLAN §48), not round 15 as GENERALISATION.md and the brief say; round 15 contains no twin material. None of it has run on two real machines: DRILL.md's 22 scenarios have an empty record template and end with "the redundancy claims in PLAN §48–§63 are claims about the simulated room the tests build", QUALIFICATION.md's 21 matrices contain no twin or node row, the field's files at rounds 76-78 come from one laptop under the watchdog, and every automated test exercises one real side against a fake peer on loopback (TwinAppTests), a fake child process (TwinLauncherTests) or a fake lease (PrimaryInstanceAppTests) — no test starts a second Patterns process. The design's core ideas are sound and well argued (a second process not a thread; one restore path shared with the watchdog restart; automatic takeover off across machines; a three-answer process read; a handover as a transaction whose shape decides who lets go first; HMAC-proved key; receipts from the switcher), but the authority semantics were reached by five successive rounds of review each finding real fail-open bugs in the previous round's "done" (34, 39 H2, 43, 45, then 78.2 on the single-machine lease), the project's own deferral rule in §63.5 (split TwinService only after a drill) was overridden at round 51, and the round-56 brief then parked the twin with five triage P0/P1 items that were never picked up. The nodes were the maintainer's ask and NODES.md judged them viable, but rounds A-D landed 11.2K lines in one day and needed a hardening round (audience phones on the control socket, the kernel never built), a kernel rewrite (40.3, 41), a frame ring (42), a Machine tab (48) and two assembly extractions (59.2, 59.7) before the maintainer's later position — single machine first, render nodes postponed until a rig — was written down. With hindsight the same-machine standby (round 32's launcher, marker and hand-back) was the right insurance because it is testable on one Windows laptop and is the shape the field actually needed at 76.1, while the cross-machine fences, the wall-switch receipts, the link clock, the arcade, the audience room and the rig-day games were a detour that consumed the rounds in which the OWN-tile take bug, the 50 Hz TV under a 60 fps show, the screencast refusal and the lease bug could only have been found by putting the single desk on a Windows machine earlier. The things worth repeating are the fail-closed, pure-Core authority types — ProcessSight, SidecarRead, TwinTransaction, TwinAuth — whose doctrine later fixed the take ticket, the wire and the field's TAKE fault, and the one-restore-path rule that the round-76 handover restart stood on.

### performance-frame-budget
The day-1 architecture (PLAN §3) promised an allocation-free per-frame path, compositor-driven redraw only while animated, and one engine for many sinks; the record shows the first two were asserted, not measured, for most of the project's life. The repository holds exactly two allocation tests (WarpGeometryPipelineTests, round 56, commit 1504426 on 2026-09-14; FrameSmootherTests, round 72.7, 656b5e8 on 09-16) among 2,429 tests, and the first of them found 1,616 B per plain frame and ~100 KB per meshed frame sixteen days after the claim was written; every earlier per-frame allocation (pre-release item 13, round 18's gradient stops and spectrum buffers, round 29's item 22) was found by reading code, and round 57's "a steady second of video allocates nothing" is a doc comment on FramePool.cs with no test. The timing fences that exist are either 60x looser than the measured number (DeskPollTests `< 100 ms` against a measured 1.2-1.6 ms, FrameBudgetAppTests `< 100 ms`, both unchanged since rounds 13-14) or assert only `>= 0` (SwitchBudget round 50, GoLatency round 52), and the quality ladder's tests feed it hand-recorded frames because "the render loop that would feed real p95s ran nowhere here" (REVIEW 56.8). What grew instead is about 40 budget/governor/ladder/fence/census/ledger classes (~6,900 lines) each fanned out to the STABILITY block, 79 super-check rows, a 26-column CSV that restarts on every column change, STATE, Companion and the Eye — added in symptom order (ladder r14, GC mode r18 before the 10.5 ms JSON clone per publish was removed at r29, lanes r56 with the audio area left unbudgeted, fence r57 rewritten r64, residency and GPU governor r69), coherent in the per-frame ordering but not designed from a measured budget. No performance number in the docs was ever measured on Windows with a GPU by the project: every figure is "on this Linux box, headless", the Windows CI lane is a Hyper-V runner at 1024x768 @ 1 Hz without the desk's perf tests, and 0 of 21 qualification rows and the round-9 master-rate checklist rows were never filled. The only real numbers are the maintainer's three reports: round 71 (desk tick 203/406 ms from a 200-400 ms COM enumeration on the UI thread every second, invisible to the Linux benchmark because WASAPI does not exist there), and rounds 77-78 (render clock 12.4-16.5 Hz on an i5-1145G7/Iris Xe, CPU 87 %, outputs 11.5-13.9 fps, ladder at level 2-3 doing nothing because it scales only particles and fractal rasters while the show was a clip plus a YouTube page captured by a 20 fps UI-thread screenshot poll). The code explains the quarter-rate render: every sink (preview, program pane, PGM and PVW tile per screen, RUN monitor, outputs) is a full engine pass on one compositor thread, a CPU fractal raster is computed per sink, and the "render clock" is measured from UI-thread animation-frame callbacks, so saturation reads as "LIMITED BY RENDER CLOCK" and blames the platform; the project's own docs (§14.2 at round 12, FractalRaster's small-laptop comment, WEB-VIDEO §1 at round 68) contained everything needed to predict it, and the scheduler that would starve monitors before outputs is still deferred at round 78. The "instant UX" and "rock solid" rounds fixed real causes (round 13's DNS and serialisation per tick, round 29's publish cost, round 56.1/56.3/56.6, round 71's catalogue) but wrapped them in governors that the field then showed governing the wrong variable.

### code-quality-sample
Read end to end: SandboxService.cs (427 lines), TakePlan.cs incl. TakeTicket/TakeLanding (342), SnapshotBus.cs (569), RenderPipeline.cs (961), WirePeer.cs (214), ShowActions.Switcher.cs (592, both the Take/Cut and ScreenTake/ScreenCut handlers fully), plus TakeTruthAppTests.cs and TakeTicketTests.cs. The core is better than average: TakePlan/TakeTicket are pure records whose Resolve/Land are tested in Core without a desk; SnapshotBus publishes by one volatile write and the published clone throws on any setter (Observable.cs:40); RenderPipeline fails visibly (black without a fence seat, last-good redraw with a rate-limited log) and disposes 16 Skia objects under the same gate as Render; WirePeer is a small, bounded single-writer queue; ShowActions.Execute converts exceptions to Failed and logs them, never to Done; all 27 pragma sites carry a reason and async void is at zero. The docs overstate it in four places. First, ActionResult is a status plus words (ShowAction.cs:588-599), so the round-78 "attempts are not facts" fix is delivered as appended sentences: the wall TAKE still returns Done with the outputs off and Done when nothing changed (Switcher.cs:206-213, 421-433) while the tile path Refuses the same no-op (268-273), and 21 of the 70 asserts in TakeTruthAppTests read message substrings. Second, size: RunSwitcher is one 298-line switch with a 93-line case that duplicates the sting handling of the case above it; DrawOutput is 174 lines with two near-identical branches; repo-wide 100 methods exceed 80 lines and 3 exceed 300, and no size rule has an entry in the generated .editorconfig even though ArchitectureFenceTests already fences per-file counts. Third, the target vocabulary is split across ContentTargets, ScreenRoles, Rig, RigGeometry and LookService with the repeater test re-implemented inline seven times and three different "what does this target show" functions, which is why rounds 67, 72, 75, 76 and 78 each re-did the take path and TakeTicket ended up with four index-aligned lists. Fourth, comments narrate rounds (34 "round N" in the 592-line Switcher) and 18 stale doubled summary blocks sit above their replacements; 78 static non-readonly fields and 70 static settable properties serve as test seams, forcing DisableTestParallelization. Correctness risks found are mostly latent rather than live: an unreachable catch in WirePeer, an ObjectDisposedException window in FlushAsync, SectionsPublished raised before _current is assigned, JSON-serialisation identity run per publish in SettledOwn, and a single-writer bus that is guarded by convention (DispatcherTimers, UiThread.InvokeAsync) with no CheckAccess on the publish path.

Switching the rules on found 145 culture-sensitive sites ('On a Hamburg desk 0.5 becomes 0,5'), 39 undisposed fields, 55 P/Invokes without a search path, 26 fire-and-forget tasks and 29 regexes without timeouts - fixed in one round. The day-1 review's mutex named by string.GetHashCode is analyzer-class. The 'results ignored' class that ANALYSIS.md calls 'an attempt taken for a fact' is the class the round-43 doctrine had to name by hand. ModuleRulesTests came at 59 and ArchitectureFenceTests at 69; the arcade loading on every node was found only when the Windows […]
- What instead: Day-1 Directory.Build.props with SonarAnalyzer.CSharp, BannedApiAnalyzers, VisualStudio.Threading.Analyzers and the generated .editorconfig (tools/gen_editorconfig.py) at error for the culture, disposal, threading, security and banned families; CultureGuard in Main; ModuleRules and ArchitectureFence tests created with the first project split; a size fence as a test (for example 1,500 lines per file, 3 partials per class) so a file is split when it crosses the line, not when a round is spent on it.
- Why it matters: Removes a 294-site round (70), three retrofit rounds (51, 59, 69) become continuous work, and a class of field-only failures (locale formatting on the wire and in show files) never accumulates. The doctrine's 'attempt taken as fact' fixes at round 43 would have been partly mechanical from the start.

Keep the one action layer with one resolver behind every surface, the evidence doctrine, the per-round REVIEW with its honest limits, tests named per unit, the claim-by-claim assessment of every critique at a named line, and the lifecycle census - and adopt the doctrine on day 1 with an observed-effect field on ActionResult

- What happened: After round 16 every desk key, cue step, wire line, OSC and Companion action is one ShowAction through one executor; TakePlan is 'the one resolver behind CUT, TAKE and the wire' (67.2), which is why 220 verbs could reach seven readers consistently. The doctrine adopted at 43 (90d6a9b) is cited through 67, 68, 69, 70, 72 and 74 and is what let round 78 name its own fault. ### V27. No governor without a measured cause and a bounded test: stop adding ladders, fences and budgets ahead of the number they act on
- Lens: performance-frame-budget · id perf-04 · Kind: change · Impact (lens): high · Confidence: medium · When: round 14 (the ladder built the same day as the budget it reads) and again at rounds 18, 50, 52, 56.7, 57, 64, 69
- What happened: About 40 budget/governor/ladder/fence/census/ledger classes (~6,900 lines) accreted in symptom order, each fanned out to the Machine page, 79 super-check rows, the CSV, STATE, Companion and the Eye (SystemMetricsService 37 commits, SuperCheck 28, RenderPipeline 37). ShowGc's sustained-low-latency mode (r18) preceded removal of the 10.5 ms JSON clone per publish (r29) and the 3x-per-GO serialisation (r56); the r14 ladder was built blind and rewritten at r56.8 on 'chosen numbers'; the r57 fence's first rule starved every pool on every preview click and was rewritten at r64; SwitchBudget (r50) and GoLatency (r52) shipped with `>= 0` assertions; r56.7's lanes left the audio area unbudgeted and i […]
- What instead: A rule for the round plan: a performance mechanism enters only with (a) a measured number from the frame-contract tests or a field CSV that names the cause, (b) a bounded test that fails without it, and (c) one reading surface (STATE) until the field asks for more. Prefer removing the cost (as r13's DNS cache, r29's section sharing, r56.3's geometry cache, r71's catalogue did) over regulating it; when a governor is still needed, design it against the measured bottleneck (sink count and UI-thread occupancy), not the effects' density.

- Lens: assistant-llm · id R5 · Kind: change · Impact (lens): medium · Confidence: medium · When: round 53, the first time a measurement block (Health/Attention) was added to the brief; re-checked at round 66
- What happened: After round 19 the brief gained, in order, devices and the twin (r31), health and attention lines (r53), the modules line (r59.8), per-screen signal truth (r65.7), the machine as Windows describes it — every GPU, display with EDID, audio endpoint (r65.9), the seven-stage commissioning flow (r65.10), and the God's Eye as 'every thing ... then every link' (r66). ### V66. Give the two inner schemas a version and a migration: the LookData string inside LookConfig.Json and the ShowState inside RecoverySnapshot.Air
- Lens: persistence-compat · id P5 · Kind: change · Impact (lens): medium · Confidence: medium · When: 2026-09-03 (v4, when looks got stable ids) for LookData; 2026-09-07 (7ee5c7d) when the recovery record began carrying a whole ShowState
- What happened: Looks - the operator's most valuable saved data - are an opaque JSON string of LookData nested in the show JSON (ShowState.cs:1054, LookService.cs:31) that migrations skip on purpose (SettingsStore.cs:161-163), so every PatternConfig rename since 09-03 has silently aged every saved look with no test. The recovery record has no version field (Resilience.cs:178-192) and its Air state is restored through Sandbox.RestoreProgram (AppServices.cs:1507-1618) without SettingsStore.Migrate (no Migrate call in Patterns.App), while the watchdog's UpdateApply makes 'old build's record, new build's reader' a normal restart sequence.
- What instead: Store looks as typed LookData objects in the show (or at least a LookData.SchemaVersion inside the string) and run them through the same gated Migrate; give RecoverySnapshot a version, pass Air through Migrate on read, and refuse a record whose version is newer than the build. Both changes are small in code and would be caught by the P1 fixtures.

### V73. Keep the encoder-host kit (ring, line protocol, job object, backoff, health words) and the crash-note/safe-run kit as the templates for every native edge
- Lens: portability-and-native-runtime · id R6 · Kind: keep · Impact (lens): medium · Confidence: high · When: round 14 (crash note, createdump, safe run) and round 16 (the host)
- What happened: The encoder host moved libVLC's encoder out with a three-slot shared-memory ring that allocates nothing per frame, a stdin/stdout word protocol with error code words (libvlc/start/encoder), a Windows job object so a dead desk never leaves an encoder streaming, the watchdog's own backoff and a six-in-ten-minutes stand-down, and status words the operator can read ('the show is untouched'). ### V80. Sign update packages and verify the signature in UpdatePackage.Inspect, for hand-dropped and management-delivered zips alike
- Lens: security-control-network · id SEC-3 · Kind: change · Impact (lens): medium · Confidence: medium · When: round 12 (8e0949c), when the update folder, the watchdog swap and the management check-in were built; at the latest round 64 when CI began producing a release m […]
- What happened: A permanent-install product whose exe is swapped by a watchdog accepts any zip with a version in patterns.update.json and Patterns.exe at its root; the folder beside the exe on a network share is declared 'the trust boundary'. The management channel authenticates the transfer (SHA-256 delivered in the same reply as the URL) and the reply (a plaintext shared-token echo compared with string.Equals, http allowed on private ranges and .local), then applies without the passcode (byPolicy: true) and restarts with the desk's own passcode. Nothing authenticates the publisher of the code that will run at the next window. No incident is recorded, but the docs never name this as remote code execution b […]
- What instead: Compile one public key into Patterns; have CI (which already builds a release manifest at round 64) sign the manifest (version + per-file SHA-256) and have UpdatePackage.Inspect refuse an unsigned or mis-signed package with a Problems line, whatever path it arrived by. Keep the SHA-256 in the management reply as a download check, require https for any non-loopback management URL (drop the private-range http carve-out), and compare the token in constant time as AdminGate already does. Roughly 60 lines plus one CI secret.
- Why it matters: It closes the one path by which a LAN peer or a compromised signage CMS ('Anything that speaks HTTP can be the server') gets code onto every machine at 03:00 without a passcode; it makes the 'permanent install' pitch in docs/INSTALLS.md honest; and it reuses the round-64 release manifest instead of adding a new artefact.

### V82. Keep the audience-port pattern: a separate listener, a route allowlist that 404s both ways, tighter limits, budgets per address, and a fuzz test with hostile paths
- Lens: security-control-network · id SEC-5 · Kind: keep · Impact (lens): medium · Confidence: high · When: rounds 37-39 (audience play, then H1 3e6827e and H6 0b28d9c on 2026-09-13)
- What happened: The only surface meant for untrusted phones was built fail-closed from its first hardening round: its own TcpListener with its own bind, AudienceRoutes.Allows as an allowlist (everything else 'Not here — the audience port answers the play pages only'), the control port refusing audience routes in return, HttpLimits.Audience tighter than the control port's, a per-address ledger, and AudienceFuzzTests that throw /api/cmd, /api/state, /../../etc/passwd, /%00 and a 3000-character path at it. Writing the fuzz exposed a bytes-vs-characters body read that would have hung on the first accented name.
- What instead: Repeat it verbatim for every future public-facing port, and apply the same allowlist discipline to the control port's read side (today /api/state, /pgm.jpg and /mv.jpg are open to any unpaired LAN peer by design).
- Why it matters: This is the one network surface with no retrofit round after its hardening and no field fault in the rounds 76-78 reports; the round-39 review records the fuzz paying for itself before the port met a phone. It is the in-repo proof that a fail-closed door costs one round when built early and four when retrofitted.

### V83. Keep the twin's key lifecycle as the template for every credential: desk-generated by default, proved by HMAC over nonces, never on the wire, held by adversarial tests
- Lens: security-control-network · id SEC-6 · Kind: keep · Impact (lens): medium · Confidence: high · When: round 34 (e491a8a, key mandatory and auto-generated) and round 44 (95c0fc7, HMAC challenge)
- What happened: The twin shipped at round 31 with an optional key sent in the JOIN and compared with string.Equals. Round 34 made a keyless main mint its own key before opening its port and refused any join without it 'even when it claims the show'; round 44 replaced the key-on-the-wire with HMAC-SHA256 proofs over fresh nonces on both sides, with tests that the key is never on the wire and that a forged join is refused. After round 44 no twin-trust retrofit appears in rounds 45-78, whereas the control port needed 65, 75 and 76.
- What instead: Nothing to change in the twin; the lesson is the contrast. The pairing token should have followed the twin's shape (generated by the desk, not opt-in), and SendSecrets should default to false when the link is not on the show's own network, since the link is authenticated but unencrypted and mirrors device passwords and the weather key in clear JSON by default.
- Why it matters: It is the repo's own evidence that a credential designed with a lifecycle (mint, prove, rotate, test the forgery) stays fixed, while a credential bolted on as a string field keeps leaking. The round-34 review's own words ('a keyless main used to hand any machine on the network the whole mirrored show (secrets among it)') describe exactly the state the control port stayed in until round 65.

### V86. Put the analyzers, the banned-API list and a de-DE test run in the day-1 build props
- Lens: testing · id R4 · Kind: change · Impact (lens): medium · Confidence: high · When: day 1 (c584687 scaffold) — Directory.Build.props existed with TreatWarningsAsErrors
- What happened: Until round 70 the build ran only the SDK's default dozen rules. Round 70's baseline found 145 culture-sensitive sites (92 CA1305, 53 CA1310 — the wire's verbs among them), 39 undisposed fields, 55 P/Invokes without a search path, 29 regexes without timeouts, 27 CA2000, 7 ignored results; all fixed in one round. The suite could never have caught the culture class: only CultureGuardTests (round 70) sets a non-invariant culture, and CI's Ubuntu runner is effectively invariant.
- What instead: SonarAnalyzer.CSharp, BannedApiAnalyzers with BannedSymbols.txt (TickCount, Thread.Sleep, GC.Collect, Task.Wait) and the culture/disposal/threading rule families as errors in Directory.Build.props from the scaffold, and a CI matrix leg that runs the Core and App suites with LANG=de_DE.UTF-8 and DOTNET_SYSTEM_GLOBALIZATION_INVARIANT=0. Fix findings as they appear (a handful per round) instead of 300 at once.

## Completeness review: gap findings G1 to G8

Four gap investigations were run after the first synthesis (the standing brief; a consolidated open-at-HEAD list; the control link's transport; the sum of the rebuilt plan). Each produced two recommendations, and each of those faced the same three skeptics. Numbering follows the document.

### G1. Make the module's lines.txt the writer's oracle the day the writer is born
- Gap: No consolidated 'found at HEAD · id R1 · Kind: change · Impact (investigator): medium · Confidence: high · When: Round 74.2 (7d473be, 2026-09-17), when WireWriter and WireWriterTests were written; the input already existed from round 54.1 (lines.txt, aebd42e)
- What happened: The recorder's round-trip test iterated the 251-row hand table WireVocabularyTests.Verbs (WireWriterTests.cs:18), which had never gained rows for AUDIO ROUTE/ROUTING/UNROUTE/VOG (round 55), COUNTDOWN FOLLOW/TOGGLE and PLAN (rounds 47 and earlier). The writer therefore shipped with 17 of 220 kinds unwritten, nine of them wire verbs, and 19 of the module's 296 lines are dropped by ControlService.cs:501 without a log. Three documents (REMOTE.md:98, PLAN.md:9696-9699, the WireWriter.cs header) claimed whole-vocabulary coverage, and CI stayed green for rounds 74-78 because the only lines.txt test (CompanionModuleContractTests.cs:29-43) checks parse, not write-back.
- What instead: One ~12-line test at round 74.2: for every line in integrations/companion-module-patterns/test/lines.txt, parse → WireWriter.Line → parse, asserting a non-empty line for every kind that is not Take/Cut/Note/Unknown/CarriesSecret and an equal action after the second parse. That would have failed on 19 lines the same afternoon and named the nine kinds. Keeping the hand table for negative cases is fine; the positive oracle must be the file the module generates, because it is the only mirror a machine writes.
- Why it matters: The recorder is an operator-facing feature (Companion's Action Recorder, a key's LEARN per REMOTE.md:347) whose failure is silence: an operator recording a break-music routing or a plan shift gets a deck page missing those presses and no error. The repo paid four rounds of a false coverage claim in three papers, and the gap survived the round-72 and round-75 critiques and the twenty-lens retrospective because no artefact ever compared the module's emitted vocabulary with the writer's.
- Evidence: src/Patterns.Core/Services/WireWriter.cs (203 of 220 kinds; '_ => ""' at :229) | integrations/companion-module-patterns/test/lines.txt:41-54,71-72,77,174-176 | src/Patterns.App/Services/ControlService.cs:500-501 | tests/Patterns.Core.Tests/WireWriterTests.cs:18; tests/Patterns.Core.Tests/WireVocabularyTests.cs:16 (251 rows) | git log -S: 3a9e0cb (round 55), 73923c2 (round 47), f863ee9 vs 7d473be (round 74.2) | docs/REMOTE.md:98; docs/PLAN.md:9692-9699
- Skeptics:
  - counterfactual: 1. "would have named the nine kinds" → "eight of the nine". lines.txt has no AUDIO UNROUTE line: the module emits it only when the route action's single checkbox `off` is set (actions.js:425), and emit-lines.mjs:15 samples a checkbox at its default (false). So the lines.txt oracle would have missed AudioUnroute. Pair the lines.txt round-trip with the repo's own enum-exhaustiveness idiom (ActionVocabularyTests.cs:19, CueStackTests.cs:58): for every ShowActionKind, either WireWriter.Line writes a non-empty line for a sample action or the kind is in a named no-wire list (Take, Cut, Note, Unknown, CueFire, ListBack, ListGo, ListReset) — that names all nine and does not depend on the module's sampling. Also have emit-lines sample both checkbox states so lines.txt carries every line the module can send (the contract test's own header promises that).
2. "Three documents claimed whole-vocabulary coverage (REMOTE.md:98, PLAN.md:9696-9699, the WireWriter.cs header)": REMOTE.md:98 says the desk pushes ACTION "for every action that succeeded from another origin" — a coverage claim by implication, not the phrase. The literal "round-trips the whole vocabulary" is in CHANGELOG.md:116-118, PLAN.md:9696 and WireWriter.cs:9-10. Say "four artefacts, one by implication".
3. Add the antecedent: the writer inherited a table already nine kinds short because the round-16 door (PLAN.md:2439-2444) was not honoured at f863ee9 (2026-09-09, CountdownToggle), 73923c2 (round 47, CountdownFollow/Plan*) and 3a9e0cb (round 55, AudioRoute/Routing/Unroute/VogMode) — each changed ControlProtocol.cs and not WireVocabularyTests.cs. The lines.txt oracle at 74.2 is the cheaper fix; a table-completeness guard would have been the earlier one.
4. Impact: state that the operator-facing failure is latent, inferred from the module code, not observed — none of the round 76-78 field files record a recorder session. "Medium, latent" is the honest rating.
5. Expectation-setting for the proposed test: its equality assertion relies on parser normalisation being idempotent for the other 277 lines (spot-checked: DECK FIRST → DeckPage "first" → "DECK PAGE first" → DeckPage "first" via Decks.cs:30; SCREEN n GROUP x → ROLE x holds via the table row 48; PVW PROGRAM → ScreenToPreview → PREVIEW holds). Any further failure it surfaced would be a real writer/parser disagreement, so the first run may show more than 19 lines, not fewer.
  - evidence: 1. WireVocabularyTests.Verbs has 245 rows (lines 18-263), not 251; it covers 197 distinct kinds. 2. AUDIO UNROUTE is not in lines.txt: the module emits it (actions.js:425) behind a checkbox, and emit-lines.mjs samples checkboxes at their default (false), so the proposed lines.txt oracle would name eight kinds, not nine, and would still miss AudioUnroute. lines.txt itself has this blind spot; the fix should also make emit-lines.mjs press each checkbox both ways. 3. The coverage claim appears in five places, not three: add CHANGELOG.md:117-118 and COMPANION-NAVIGATOR.md:175 to WireWriter.cs:9, REMOTE.md:98 and PLAN.md:9696-9699. 4. The cited range lines.txt:41-54 includes line 51 (AUDIO STOP), which is written; the dropped count in that range is 13, and 19 overall is still right. 5. The proposed test must skip the eight non-action lines in lines.txt (PING at 170, MENU LOOK Walk-in at 136, NAV DECK x6 at 155-160, which parse to Ping/Menu/NavDeck RemoteCommandKinds) via IsAction; with that, the round trip of the remaining shapes is already exercised by the same writer and parser in the 245-row table, so the "would have failed on 19 lines" prediction is credible, though it could not be executed here (no .NET SDK). 6. The operator scenario is narrower than stated: desk-side audio crosspoints call AudioRouting.SetRoute/ClearRoute directly (AudioPage.cs:233, 237, 249) and desk-side plan shifts call CueStack.ShiftPlan directly (RunViewModel.cs:137-138), neither passing through ShowActions.Execute or the Performed event, so a recorder would not see those presses even with a complete writer. What the writer gap does silently drop is AUDIO ROUTING on/off from the desk's Audio page (AudioPage.cs:39, origin Desk) and all nine verbs when they arrive from any non-automation origin other than the recording connection — another deck, OSC, MIDI-learned lines, HTTP, the assistant — which REMOTE.md:98 promises are fed. 7. CountdownToggle entered the parser at round 20 (f863ee9, 2026-09-09), CountdownFollow and PLAN at round 47 (73923c2); "rounds 47 and earlier" is accurate but could be stated precisely. 8. CompanionModuleContractTests: the test spans lines 28-42 ([Fact] at 28), not 29-43. 9. The checkout's HEAD is 30e1463 (adds docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md only); all source citations were verified identical to 08dcaaa.

Rewrite why_it_matters to: 'No retrofit has been paid — the nine kinds are still unwritten at HEAD (WireWriter.cs has two commits, the second only adding the CarriesSecret guard); the cost so far is a latent silent drop and a false coverage sentence in CHANGELOG.md:117, COMPANION-NAVIGATOR.md:153, PLAN.md:9696 and the WireWriter/WireWriterTests headers, held for about 24 hours (rounds 74.2-78.5). The round-72 critique predates the writer and could not have caught this; the round-75 critique did look at the recorder (pairing, secrets) and missed coverage.'
