Priority: a do-it-anyway item because the cost is ~12-15 lines in CompanionModuleContractTests.cs (RepoRoot/ModuleDir already there) and the pattern generalises to OscMap and the menus; it is not a top-tier finding beside the round 76-78 field defects.

### G2. Close every round's papers with a code-lens 'found, still open' ledger, kept apart from the field's next list
- Gap: No consolidated 'found at HEAD · id R2 · Kind: change · Impact (investigator): medium · Confidence: high · When: From round 61 (2026-09-15), when CHANGELOG.md, tags and releases gave each round a formal close; at the latest round 65, when the ADRs were written retrospectively
- What happened: Each round's PLAN section ends with a 'what is next' written from the field's or the maintainer's questions (PLAN §96.8: six items, all field-facing). Defects found by reading code but not fixed in the round have no home, so they vanish: the tracked 3.3.0 tgz (since round 60.4), DescriptorVersion never read by its one client (since round 75), the support bundle lacking playhead.json and quality.json (since round 76), SamePicture's JSON identity per publish (round 78), the FrameInput two-read pairing, the SectionsPublished-before-assign ordering, WirePeer's dead catch and FlushAsync window, and ADR-008 describing a lane four files use while eleven writers roll their own temp-then-move. The interim retrospective inherits the same shape: it scatters the schema rule, createdump/registry and fail-open items inside hindsight entries and omits the rest, so a maintainer opening round 79 has no single list.
- What instead: A short docs/OPEN.md (or a fixed final subsection of each PLAN round) with one row per item: file:line, round found, severity, and the round that closed it — appended by the agent whenever a review or a lens finds something it does not fix, and read at the start of the next round beside 'what the files said'. Ten to fifteen rows at round 78, maintained in minutes. The papers' existing discipline (REVIEW.md 'what was found on the way') already produces the rows; it lacks only the column 'still open' and a place that survives the round.
- Why it matters: Under a solo maintainer directing an agent, anything not in the next round's opening prompt is effectively forgotten: the record shows items that stayed open for 4 to 18 rounds without a mention, while items on a field list were closed within one round (round 78's four fixes all came from §96.1). The cost paid is not any single defect's severity but that the cheapest, most certain fixes in the repository — a git rm, a 2-file bundle addition, a 10-line test — are the ones nobody has done, because the process that finds them has no ledger for what it does not fix.
- Evidence: docs/PLAN.md:10368-10386 (§96.8 six field items, no code item) | docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md: no open-items section; §4 items 8/12/13 at :168-216 scatter live items; zero hits for WireWriter, tgz, DescriptorVersion, SamePicture, SupportBundle, playhead | git log 58e07f0 (tgz tracked round 60.4) vs .gitignore aebd42e (round 54.1); src/Patterns.Core/Services/ControlProtocol.cs:1419 vs integrations/companion-module-patterns/src/nav.js:35,127 | src/Patterns.Core/Services/RemoteAdmin.cs:76-83 vs PlayheadRecord.cs:26 and QualityLadder.cs:35 | docs/ADR.md:131-146 vs the eleven direct-writer files and SettingsStore.cs:394 (WriteAtomic called only in SettingsStore)
- Skeptics:
  - evidence: 1. "Each round's PLAN section ends with a 'what is next'" and "defects ... have no home" are overstated. Only 13 rounds have a "What the round did not do (and what is next)" subsection, starting at §86.8 (round 68); before that, 25 rounds carry "Left for later" / "Considered and left" subsections (§54.6 round 36 through §71.4 round 53). So a per-round home for deferrals existed in ~38 rounds; what it lacks is exactly what R2 proposes — it holds design deferrals rather than code-lens defects, has no file:line or "still open" column, and is never re-read (e.g. §54.6/§60.5's "the arcade's own sound is still none" is never referenced again). Reword to "no cumulative home; the per-round 'Considered and left' and 'did not do' lists are neither code-lens nor carried forward".
2. "the support bundle lacking playhead.json and quality.json (since round 76)": SupportBundle.Files was last changed at f111fc9 (round 65.9); RemoteAdmin.cs was touched again at 1626710 (round 76.6, secret masking) without adding either file. playhead.json was born at ce3641f (round 76.1), so "since round 76" holds for it; quality.json has existed since a0dc647 (2026-09-06, rounds 15-17 by CHANGELOG dates), so the bundle has lacked it for roughly 60 rounds, not since 76.
3. "items that stayed open for 4 to 18 rounds": by the recommendation's own dating the span is 3 (DescriptorVersion, 75→78) to 18 (tgz, 60→78); with quality.json's real age it is up to ~60. 
4. ADR-008's decision text scopes the lane to "the show's files — autosaves, the recovery record's writes and clears, the final save", and PersistenceRuntime wraps only SettingsStore and RecoveryStore. The eleven sidecar writers therefore do not contradict ADR-008; the item is a duplication/consistency observation (a shared WriteAtomic exists and is used by four file kinds in SettingsStore only), not a defect. Of the eleven File.Move files, two are not temp-then-move JSON writers (OutputOwnership.cs:258 is an ISidecarFiles.Move seam; DeckConverter.cs:182 is a cache move); the clean temp-then-move sites are ten in nine files (Resilience.cs:245 and :530, QualityLadder.cs:60, ShowLog.cs:153, SpotifyCredentialStore.cs:50, RemoteAdmin.cs:339, PlayheadRecord.cs:54, ManagementService.cs:174, KnownGoodRig.cs:46, Assistant.cs:72).
5. §96.8 does contain code work — item 1 (a flag on TakeTarget) and item 4 (a policy-key fallback) — so "no code item" should read "no code-lens item; all six are field-derived".
6. The eight examples were surfaced by the retrospective's own lenses; the record shows only that nothing ever recorded them, not that the agent "found" them during a round and dropped them. State the claim as "nothing in the process records a code finding it does not fix" rather than "found ... vanish".
7. Overlap: RETROSPECTIVE §4 item 10 already proposes a per-round docs/rounds/NN.md holding "found on the way", and §7 point 4 repeats it. R2's distinct contribution is the "still open" column, the file:line row and the start-of-round read; present it as an amendment to item 10, not a separate ledger, or the retrospective will carry two overlapping process items.
8. Add the missed severity fact for the tgz row: CI's `*.tgz` glob at the module root (build.yml:95) plus companion-module-build writing to that root (test/package.test.mjs:55) and `cp release/module/*.tgz release/out/` (build.yml:306) means the stale 3.3.0 package rides into every Release artifact beside the current one (unverified against actual release assets: API 404).
9. docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md exists only on branch claude/retrospective-hindsight at 30e1463, not at 08dcaaa on claude/nice-hopper-vf1paa; say so when citing it.
  - counterfactual (REFUTED this one): Reword from "create a code-lens 'found, still open' ledger" to: "Keep REVIEW.md's 'The review's items, answered' subsection (rounds 64-76) as a non-optional close of every round, including field rounds 77 and 78 where it was dropped for 'the files read'; make it cumulative rather than restated (every 'Seen, not done' bullet from the round is added; nothing leaves the list until a row names the closing unit, as 72.3 -> 75.3 did); give each row a file:line. The lapse cost is small and specific: round 75's support-bundle question (REVIEW:2750) was not carried into round 76's list and rounds 77-78 have no list at all." Also correct: (1) the eight cited items were never found by any round's review, so the ledger alone would not have captured them — state that a per-round code-lens pass is the real added cost, and lower confidence from high to medium; (2) replace "4 to 18 rounds" with "1 to 3 calendar days (09-15 to 09-18)"; (3) drop or rewrite the ADR-008 row: the ADR covers the show file and recovery record through PersistenceRuntime, not sidecar atomic writes; if kept, phrase it as "a shared atomic-write helper (SettingsStore.WriteAtomic) that eleven sidecar writers duplicate", not as an ADR violation; (4) note that two of round 78's four fixes came from the maintainer's handed-in code assessment, which shows code-lens items close within a round when they reach the opening prompt — the lever is the opening prompt, which the existing subsection already fed; (5) add that the tracked 3.3.0 tgz matters slightly more than stated: the companion-module job uploads integrations/companion-module-patterns/*.tgz and the release step copies release/module/*.tgz, so every Release since round 61 plausibly ships the stale 3.3.0 beside the current module; (6) fold the recommendation into the retrospective's item 10 (per-round files with "found on the way") rather than presenting it as a separate change, since the "when" of round 61 is already answered by the subsection appearing at round 64.
  - cost: Impact: low (not medium). Confidence: medium (not high) — the 'no home' claim and the 'REVIEW already produces the rows' claim are both wrong on the record. Rescope 'what_happened': the papers do record unfixed finds ('Seen, not done' x5, 'Measured, and left' x10, '— left' x12 in REVIEW; 'What the round did not do' in 7 PLAN rounds; deferred rows in PLAN §83.14 and ROADMAP-ASSESSMENT 'Closed'), and the one code-lens item recorded that way (round-72.3 LOCK) was closed at round 75.2; the eight items cited were never found by any round, so a ledger would not have contained them. Rescope 'what_instead' to two separate things with different costs: (a) at each papers commit, copy every 'Seen, not done' / 'left' / 'did not do' bullet into one fixed table (docs/OPEN.md or a PLAN appendix) with the round found and the round closed — minutes, low impact, an index of what exists; (b) the thing that would actually have produced the cited rows is a periodic read-only code-lens pass between field reports (the kind of read the retrospective did at round 78.5), which is a new activity with a real cost per round that R2 neither proposes nor measures and should be a separate recommendation. Correct the dates: quality.json has been absent from the support bundle since 2026-09-06 (a0dc647), not since round 76. Drop 'round 78's four fixes all came from §96.1' as evidence for the ledger — it shows field files drive priority, which a code-lens ledger would not change.

### G3. Make the standing brief say 'the rig considered at each step', not 'the wire and Companion'
- Gap: The standing brief as the brea · id brief-rig-at-each-step · Kind: change · Impact (investigator): high · Confidence: high · When: Round 15 (2026-09-06), the first round whose papers read the round against the brief and whose own 'Next, ranked, 1' was the laptop's checklist row; re-applied at rounds 56 and 62 when the brief's tail was rewritten
- What happened: The maintainer's re-typed brief grew from 'stability ... instant' (rounds 13-15) to 'AI at each step, Companion always considered' (round 56) to 'instant, stable, modular, the wire and Companion considered at each step, the assistant told' (round 62), and never named an output, a display or a file from a machine. The agent honoured it as seven readers per verb: AudioFollow landed in 38 files (16 src, 8 Companion module, 5 tests, 9 papers) with Companion 3.10.0, OSC, cue kind, Eye, STATE and help on 09-16 and was wrong at the HDMI output on 09-17 (PLAN §94.5); the Companion module went through 36 versions, 21 of them on a base the current Companion could not load (PLAN 6825-6831), then 15 in four days; REMOTE.md took 97 commits and ControlProtocol.cs 67 while FramePacer.cs took 4 and DisplayModes.cs 3; and TAKE, the verb carried to every surface since round 15, journaled Done eleven times with the outputs off on the one real laptop (PLAN §96.1). The laptop existed from round 14 (two access-violation crashes), its show log from 09-03 and Super Check from 09-05, yet its first file was read at round 71 (09-16).
- What instead: From round 15, the brief's closing sentence reads: 'instant, stable; the rig considered at each step — the round closes with one row from the show laptop's showlog.jsonl, one line of patterns.log or one Super Check row for the round's headline verb, and a verb reaches the wire, Companion and the assistant only after its effect has been read once from an output.' The maintainer types this sentence anyway every round; the four files already exist beside the exe; the agent already knows how to read them (rounds 71, 77, 78 did it in a day each). Companion and the assistant become readers a verb earns after it has landed once, not surfaces it is born on; the module ships a version per round at most, and only against a Companion that has imported it.
- Why it matters: Under the original constraints — a Linux agent with no Windows, GPU, displays or Companion — the maintainer's laptop was the only source of rig truth, and the standing brief was the only channel the maintainer used on every round. Putting the requirement there cost nothing and would have surfaced the take-with-outputs-off fault, the sound on the wrong output, the 50 Hz TV and the unloadable module weeks earlier, before each was replicated across seven readers, 109KB of wire reference and 36 module versions that then had to be kept in step. The retrospective's thesis is 'evidence and scope, not stack'; this sentence is where the scope came from.
- Evidence: docs/PLAN.md:2253-2257, 7063-7066, 7913-7914 (the brief's three tails) | docs/PLAN.md:9478-9479 (seven readers), 9103-9123 (§87.5), §94.5 (the sound on every routed output) | git log -p integrations/companion-module-patterns/package.json (36 versions); docs/PLAN.md:6825-6831 (module could not be loaded), 7222-7226 (3.0.0 released three times) | git log --follow counts: REMOTE.md 97, ControlProtocol.cs 67, OscMap.cs 26 vs FramePacer.cs 4, OutputWindow.cs 9, OutputWindowManager.cs 23, DisplayModes.cs 3 | docs/PLAN.md:10222-10235 (§96.1 eleven takes Done, outputs off) | docs/PLAN.md:1061-1063 (round 14 crashes), 2304-2306 (§20.12 next step 1), 9277 (round 71 first files); 0c2c219 (showlog 09-03), 26879db (Super Check 09-05); no CHECKLIST ever ticked
- Skeptics:
  - evidence: 1) 'when': round 15 was not the first round to read the brief against the round nor the first whose 'Next, ranked, 1' was the laptop's checklist row. Round 13 §16.4 (PLAN 1569-1580, 'The brief, read as an engineer ... where Patterns stands against each') already did the former, and round 14 §18.10 (PLAN 1933-1936, 'Next, ranked. 1. The laptop's dump. Row 2 of the checklist on the machine the report came from') already did the latter. Round 15 is the first with a section titled 'the paramount list, read against the round' (§20.11); say that, or anchor at round 14 (also 09-06). 2) The AudioFollow file count is pattern-dependent: 'AudioFollow' alone hits 16 files; 'audio_follow|AudioFollow|AUDIO FOLLOW|audio/follow' hits 36 (16 src, 8 Companion module, 4 tests, 6 docs + CHANGELOG + README); adding 'FollowPicture|follows the picture' hits 47. The '38 (16/8/5/9)' is in range but not reproducible from one grep, and the established facts say 32; say 'three dozen or so files' or state the pattern. 3) The HDMI detail is in §95.1 (round 77, PLAN 10071: 'the clip played to the default device, which is the TV's HDMI'), not §94.5; §94.5 (76.3) is 'heard on every output with a route'. Both are 09-17, so cite both. 4) File names: ShowLog.cs:27 writes 'patterns.showlog.jsonl' (not showlog.jsonl); SuperCheck.cs:240 writes 'supercheck.txt'. The proposed sentence names three files, but the text then says 'the four files already exist' — name the fourth (recovery/quality) or say three. 5) 'The maintainer types this sentence anyway every round' is asserted by the papers ('The brief that closes every report', 'The standing brief as always') but recorded in PLAN only at rounds 13, 15, 56 and 62 and in three commit messages; phrase it as 'the brief the papers say closed every report'. 6) TAKE existed from a204d41 (2026-08-30, 'Switcher UX: program/preview panes, CUT/TAKE'); round 15 §20.6 added the four scopes — say 'carried to every surface from round 15's scopes on' or 'since day 2'. 7) 'no CHECKLIST ever ticked' should read 'no run of any checklist is recorded in the repository' — the tables have no result column or tick boxes, so a run done offline would leave no trace either way.
  - counterfactual: Keep the recommendation; reword as follows. (a) Replace "cost nothing" with: "costs the round cadence — rounds close at the rig's pace (one or two a day, several units each) instead of the 7-11 a day of 09-13 to 09-16; that is the intended effect, not a side effect." (b) Add an escape clause to the sentence: "...for the round's headline verb, when the round has one an output can show; a round without one says so in its papers" (about 33 of rounds 15-78 had none). (c) Replace "weeks earlier" with: "days to two weeks earlier: the outputs-off Done from any laptop run after 09-03; the module's base from the first import into the Companion the maintainer actually ran; AudioFollow and the tile's own-picture fault within a day or two of landing rather than after they had reached every reader; the 50 Hz TV the first time that TV was attached, since Super Check has flagged a display slower than the master rate since 09-05." (d) Replace "its first file was read at round 71" with "its files were first sent whole at round 71; before that the desk's evidence arrived as quoted lines and exit codes (rounds 14, 17, 55, 62)". (e) In why_it_matters, do not say the brief's tail produced the seven readers; say the every-reader discipline came from the maintainer's round-16 brief and round 54's "total control" via Companion, and that this sentence would reverse that priority for a verb's first round. (f) Make the enforceable half explicit: "the agent holds a new verb in ActionSpec.DeskOnly with the reason 'not yet read from an output' until the row arrives; the wire, OSC, Companion, the Eye and the assistant take it the round after" — the mechanism exists since round 16 — and note that round 72 item 13 and 19 unticked checklists show the sentence alone did not bind. (g) Replace the FramePacer/DisplayModes pairing with the aggregate: 73 commits touching rendering, output-window and platform files against 143 touching the wire, OSC, the module and REMOTE.md. (h) Reword "a base the current Companion could not load" to "a base the Companion the maintainer turned out to be running (5.0.5 at round 57) could not load". (i) Add to the sentence "one row read beside the output it names", since the take row itself read Done and lied. (j) AudioFollow file count is grep-pattern dependent (I get 16 src, 4 tests, 8 module, 9 papers = 37 with a broad pattern); cite the pattern or round it to "about 35 files".
  - cost: Impact: medium (not high). Confidence: medium. Rewrite the claim as: (1) the sentence costs nothing but the per-round row does not - at 7-11 rounds a day (09-13..09-16) the realistic unit is one rig session per day, and the reader gate should be 'before the next day's rounds', not 'before the wire'; (2) the plan must state the maintainer's commitment explicitly, because the agent already asked for this at round 14 (the dump), round 15 ('Next, ranked 1'), in 19 checklists, 21 qualification rows and 22 drills and got nothing until a complaint arrived at 71/77/78 - the binding constraint was the maintainer's laptop time, not the wording of the brief; (3) drop 'weeks earlier' for the take fault (2 days old, round 67 to 78) and the audio fault (1-3 days), keep it only for the Companion module (08-30 to 09-14); drop the 50 Hz TV as a saving until its fix exists (deferred in §96); (4) drop 'replicated across seven readers ... that then had to be kept in step' - the fixes (3cdf997: 5 files; e175928: 12 files; 8794153: 28 files) touched no reader; the seven-readers cost is per-verb propagation (50 files for AudioFollow, 23 for TAKE NEXT) which the gate defers by a round rather than removes; (5) state the retrofit actually paid: rounds 71+76+77+78 = 19 commits, +7,137/-413 lines (~2.4% of code written), of which the avoidable part is the 54.1 Companion re-port (+4681/-2694 in the module dir) plus round 57's release discipline and ~0.8K lines of rule fixes; (6) promote the cheapest specific rule to the headline: 'no Companion module version ships until a current Companion has imported it' (first import evidence: round 57, 09-14), and 'a rig report comes as patterns.log / showlog.jsonl / Super Check rows, not words' (rounds 14, 15, 55, 62 and 76 were words while all three files existed).

### G4. Keep reading each round against the brief, but only for items with a measurement, and name the requester in the round's opener
- Gap: The standing brief as the brea · id keep-brief-reading-with-measurable-items-and-named-requester · Kind: keep · Impact (investigator): medium · Confidence: medium · When: Round 13 (§16.4) and round 15 (§20.11), where the ritual began; the requester column from round 9, and in CHANGELOG.md's framing at round 61
- What happened: The per-round 'read against the brief' ritual worked when the brief item could be measured: §16.4's 'where it is still short' list became round 14's frame budget, start-up budget and quality ladder (PLAN 1077, 1079 cite '§16.4's first, third, fourth and fifth next steps'), and 'instant' is fenced by DeskPollTests/TickBudgetTests. It produced only surface when the item could not be measured: 'Companion always considered' yielded 15 module versions in four days with no Companion to load them in (round 58: hardware qualification 'needs a capture card, a Companion and a night'); 'AI at each step' yielded prompt text (round 32 item 6). Meanwhile the record never says who asked: every request was the maintainer's own list, critique or laptop, yet CHANGELOG.md:3 frames the rounds as 'one request from the field' and the round-12 'field research' is indexed web excerpts (FIELD-RESEARCH.md 13-15) whose fourteen problems contain none of the seven faults the real laptop produced.
- What instead: Keep §20.11/§16.4 as the closing section of every round, with the rule that a brief item stays only while it has a fence or a measurement (a tick budget, a frame budget, a Super Check row, a module import on a real Companion) and is dropped or rewritten otherwise. Add one line to every round opener and CHANGELOG entry naming the requester and the evidence class: 'the maintainer's list', 'a handed-in critique', 'the show laptop's files', 'web research'. Retitle FIELD-RESEARCH.md as desk research and drop 'from the field' from CHANGELOG until a second operator or a machine's file is cited.
- Why it matters: The ritual is the one place the repository already reasons about its own scope, and it demonstrably converted measurable brief items into lasting fences; discarding it would lose that. But without a requester column the papers let a single maintainer's lists and a proxy-blocked web search read as operator demand, so the retrospective could not tell which rounds answered whom, and the unmeasurable tail of the brief ran unchecked for 47 rounds. The cost paid is the 6,758-line module kept in step across 36 versions, the 300-line research paper that missed every real fault, and a CHANGELOG whose thesis sentence is untrue.

### G5. Gate at the router on the origin's proof, prove the token the way the twin proves its key, and say in one line what the wire leaks
- Gap: The control link's transport s · id SEC-1 · Kind: change · Impact (investigator): high · Confidence: high · When: Round 65.5 (778003d, 2026-09-15) at the latest — TwinAuth (95c0fc7, 09-13) and TwinKeys (e491a8a, 09-12) already existed; ideally with the first listener on day 2 (e43e28b, 08-30), when the same rule would have caught the admin passcode riding RESTART/UPDATE APPLY lines from round 12.
- What happened: The pairing token was designed inside a wire-plumbing round as AUTH <token> on TCP and X-Patterns-Token on HTTP, both plaintext, gated at five sites inside ControlService rather than at CommandRouter.ExecuteAsync where every origin arrives with an ActionOrigin. OSC (OscService.cs:216-217) therefore runs any mapped verb — OUTPUTS OFF, BLACKOUT, SCREEN n TAKE — from any UDP sender, binds IPAddress.Any ignoring Control.Bind, and is painted green by Super Check and the God's Eye once a token is set. Empty token is open by default, the opposite of the twin's auto-generated key. Four places claim the token is mirrored to the twin; the Control section never travels, so paired remotes lose pairing exactly at a failover. Separately, the admin passcode rode protocol lines and sat in showlog.jsonl in clear from round 12 to round 75, costing three fix commits (1d4238c, 1626710) and a permanent boot-time scrub. No document states the threat model; tests fence the per-listener behaviour, so the OSC bypass is the tested behaviour.
- What instead: (1) One predicate in CommandRouter.ExecuteAsync: a mutating ShowActionKind runs only if the ActionOrigin's connection or endpoint holds a proof; ControlService, OscService and DeviceService stop deciding trust themselves. (2) Reuse TwinAuth.Proof/Verify (Twin.cs:169-176): HELLO returns a nonce, the client answers AUTH <hmac(token, nonce|name)>; the pages fetch a nonce with GET / and send X-Patterns-Proof; the token never crosses the wire and a captured line replays nothing — one-line changes in main.js:135 and the tok()/pair() page functions. (3) OSC: bind it to Control.Bind (OscService.cs:110, one line) and either require a per-datagram HMAC with a timestamp over address+args, or keep it ungated but make Super Check's REMOTE row and the Eye's OSC edge amber 'OSC open, no pairing' whenever OscEnabled is on. (4) Treat credentials as never-an-action-argument from the start: RESTART and UPDATE APPLY authenticate by the connection's proof, not by a passcode in the line — this removes CarriesSecret, the journal blanking and ScrubSecrets entirely. (5) Fix or drop the 'mirrored to the twin' sentence and add one test: a paired deck stays paired across a takeover, or the Machine page says it will not. (6) One paragraph in REMOTE.md Trust and ADR-003: 'the link is plaintext; a sniffer on the show network learns the token unless proof mode is on; OSC is not gated; what leaves the machine is listed in Secrets.' Total: under 200 lines and two tests at round 65, versus the three later fix commits, the scrub, and a bypass that still ships.
- Why it matters: The product presents a TRUST block, a green 'paired' row, an ADR that says 'every mutating verb' is guarded, and a God's Eye whose lights a venue technician reads as safety. A verb that turns the outputs off or takes a picture to a wall from any UDP sender on the venue network is safety-relevant, and the gap is invisible to the operator by construction. The concrete cost paid: 13 days (and counting) of an ungated OSC verb path; 12 days of the admin passcode in the journal plus a scrub every future install runs at boot; a false redundancy promise in four places; and five plus one tests that lock in the wrong invariant, so a rebuild cannot rely on the suite to tell it the gate moved.
- Evidence: src/Patterns.Devices/OscService.cs:110, :216-217 | src/Patterns.App/Services/ControlService.cs:347-358, :389, :415, :451-458, :768, :872, :1079 | src/Patterns.App/Services/CommandRouter.cs:47; src/Patterns.Core/Model/ShowAction.cs:525-541 | src/Patterns.Core/Services/Twin.cs:152-176 (TwinAuth), :216-221 (TwinKeys), :314-318 (LocalSections) | src/Patterns.Core/Services/PairingToken.cs:10-12; ShowState.cs:2247-2249; docs/REMOTE.md:36-39; docs/ADR.md:41-46 | src/Patterns.Core/Services/SuperCheck.cs:961-964; src/Patterns.Core/Services/Eye.cs:649-650, :674-677 | src/Patterns.Core/Services/ActionSpec.cs:102-110; src/Patterns.Core/Services/ShowLog.cs:95-126; docs/PLAN.md:9883-9888; commits 8e0949c, 7d473be, 1d4238c, 1626710 | tests/Patterns.App.Tests/RemoteTrustTests.cs (5 tests), tests/Patterns.App.Tests/OscAppTests.cs:62 (1 test) | commits e43e28b (08-30), bc06423 (09-05), 88c8c77 (09-12), e491a8a (09-12), 95c0fc7 (09-13), 778003d (09-15)
- Skeptics:
  - evidence: 1. Replace "No document states the threat model" with: the threat model is stated but scattered and not surfaced in the app — REMOTE.md Trust block (lines 26-42) says an unset token means anyone on the network controls the show ("the trust model of most stage-control protocols") and that "OSC has no session to pair: leave it off, or keep it behind the control network"; INSTALLS.md:87-90 says the passcode "travels on the LAN in the clear like every remote command: it is a fence around a site, not a secret across the internet"; INSTALLS.md:190 says the passcode and token are "in the clear — the folder is the trust boundary". What is genuinely missing: ADR-003 is silent on OSC and plaintext; nothing names the pairing token as sniffable; the in-app Help Remote body (HelpBodies.cs:36) mentions OSC only as "the same verbs work ... over OSC"; and Super Check/Eye never repeat the REMOTE.md caveat. Item (6) should be reframed as consolidating and surfacing, not authoring from nothing. 2. "Four places claim the token is mirrored to the twin" — three were found: PairingToken.cs:11-12, ShowState.cs:2247, REMOTE.md:36-37 (grep for "mirrored to the twin" and token/twin variants across src, docs, README, CHANGELOG, Help, ViewModels, axaml). 3. "tests fence the per-listener behaviour, so the OSC bypass is the tested behaviour" / "five plus one tests that lock in the wrong invariant" — overstated. OscAppTests.cs:62 never sets Control.Token (only OscEnabled at :77/:117), so it tests OSC in open mode and would pass unchanged if OSC were gated. RemoteTrustTests' five tests assert wire-observable replies (ERR not paired, 403, closed after five wrong) and touch ControlService only through the TrustLoopback and WrongTokensBeforeClose knobs, so a gate moved to the router would need those knobs carried over but not the tests rewritten. The accurate statement is: no test asserts what OSC does when a token is set, so the OSC gap is untested rather than tested-in. 4. ADR-003 (ADR.md:43-44) says the token "guards every mutating verb over TCP and HTTP" — the qualifier is present; the paraphrase "every mutating verb" drops it. 5. "invisible to the operator by construction" should be softened: OscEnabled defaults to false (ShowState.cs:2277) and REMOTE.md tells the operator to leave it off; the real defect is that once it is on, Super Check's REMOTE row and the Eye's OSC node stay green with no caveat. 6. Feasibility/cost of (2): the Companion module (Node) can HMAC in one line, but the phone/run/stage/pad pages are served over plain HTTP from the desk's LAN address with no TLS anywhere in ControlService*, and browsers expose crypto.subtle only in secure contexts (HTTPS or localhost) — so the page side needs an inline JS HMAC-SHA256 (~80-120 lines) or the pages keep sending the token; "one-line changes in the tok()/pair() page functions" and "under 200 lines" are under-costed for the web path. 7. "ControlService, OscService and DeviceService stop deciding trust themselves" — DeviceService does not decide trust; it runs a configured device's lines unconditionally through the router with OriginKind.Device (DeviceService.cs:736-746). Under proposal (1) devices would need an implicit proof-by-configuration, which should be said. 8. Failover nuance: ControlService.cs:360-362 answers AUTH with "open — this desk asks for no token" when the token is empty, so on a standby with no token paired remotes keep working ungated; only a standby with a different token drops them. Either way the mirrored claim is false, but the consequence is "pairing lost or silently downgraded", not always lost. 9. "three fix commits (1d4238c, 1626710)" — the third is 778003d (65.5, "the passcode out of every URL"); name it. 10. Minor: the ControlService gate sites number six if line 768 (where `paired` is computed for HTTP) is counted separately from 872 and 1079.
  - counterfactual: 1. Title/what_instead (4): do not fold the admin passcode into the pairing proof. PLAN.md §83.14 deliberately keeps three tiers (open reads, paired mutating verbs, admin passcode) and the reason stands: a paired FOH deck must not be able to UPDATE APPLY or RESTART an install. Reword to: 'the admin tier stays, but it becomes a connection standing (an ADMIN proof once per connection, like the X-Patterns-Pass header already is on HTTP) instead of a verb argument; that alone removes CarriesSecret, the journal blanking and ScrubSecrets.' The strongest date for this rule is round 12 (8e0949c, 09-05: RESTART <passcode> and UPDATE APPLY <passcode> added two days after the journal existed, 0c2c219 09-03), not day 2.
2. what_instead (3): drop the per-datagram HMAC option. OSC was built for QLab, TouchOSC, Resolume and TouchDesigner (PLAN.md:4557, OriginKind.Osc comment), none of which can sign datagrams; a signed OSC is a different protocol. Keep only: bind OSC to Control.Bind (one line, the ControlConfig it already reads has Bind) and make Super Check's REMOTE row and the Eye's OSC node amber 'OSC open, no pairing' when OscEnabled is on. If any gating is wanted, the QLab-style '/connect <passcode>' per-source-address session is the interoperable shape.
3. what_instead (2) and 'Total: under 200 lines': the page side is not one line. The pages are served over plain HTTP and crypto.subtle is unavailable in non-secure contexts, so an X-Patterns-Proof needs an inline JS SHA-256/HMAC (a few hundred lines) plus per-client nonce state on the server, and after proof the pages still need a session credential that a sniffer can replay within its life. State the honest cost (roughly 400-800 lines across C#, the pages, module 3.5.0 and its fake desk, docs) and the honest benefit (the long-lived token is never disclosed; an active attacker on the same plaintext LAN is still not stopped).
4. what_instead (1): the router gate is a default-deny for new origin kinds, not the whole gate. /api/stage/ack and /api/arcade/key (ControlService.cs:872) and the RECORD / NAV DECK standings (:393-398) are not router verbs, so the listener keeps a per-connection Paired standing and passes it on the ActionOrigin; reword 'ControlService ... stop deciding trust themselves' to 'the listener establishes the standing, the router enforces it for every verb'.
5. Priority order: for this project the cheap, high-value fix is item (3) (OSC bind plus honest lights) and item (5) (the mirror sentence), because an ungated UDP port needs no capture, while stealing a token on a switched or WPA2 venue LAN needs one. Put proof mode third.
6. what_happened: 'Four places claim the token is mirrored' is three (PairingToken.cs:11-12, ShowState.cs:2247, REMOTE.md:36-37). 'Paired remotes lose pairing exactly at a failover' should read 'a standby with a different token refuses them; a standby with no token is open to them and to everyone else; the sentence is false either way'. 'No document states the threat model' should read 'ADR-003 Why and REVIEW 65.5's honest limit state a venue-LAN model for TCP/HTTP; nothing states OSC's exposure in the health lights, and REMOTE.md:38-39's advice to keep OSC behind the control network cannot be followed because OSC ignores Bind'. 'The gap is invisible to the operator by construction' should read 'invisible in Super Check and the Eye; stated in the REMOTE.md Trust block and the Remote page's OSC tooltip'.
7. why_it_matters / tests: replace 'five plus one tests lock in the wrong invariant' with 'RemoteTrustTests fence the wire's observable refusals (which a router gate would preserve) and OscAppTests never sets a token, so the missing test is OSC-with-a-token-set, not a wrong one'. Also note OSC is off by default (ShowState.cs:2277), so the '13 days' exposure applies to shows that switched it on.
8. when: 'ideally day 2 (e43e28b)' is hindsight; on 08-30 the remote drove a test-pattern tool. Keep 'round 65.5 at the latest' for the token/proof and add 'round 12 for the credential-as-standing rule', citing REVIEW.md:445-447 (round 34, 09-12) as the moment the maintainer wrote down that the remote was in the clear and an HMAC challenge would cost little.
  - cost: Impact: medium, not high. Keep the recommendation but restate it: (1) The router predicate and the amber lights are the payload; proof mode is optional and not one-line — the four embedded page scripts run over plain HTTP where crypto.subtle is unavailable, so budget an inline HMAC-SHA256 (~150 lines) or drop item (2) and state "plaintext token on a trusted VLAN" as the accepted model. (2) Replace item (3)'s per-datagram HMAC with the bind-to-Control.Bind line plus Super Check REMOTE amber "OSC open, no pairing" and an amber Eye edge when OscEnabled; QLab, TouchOSC and lighting desks cannot sign datagrams. (3) Narrow item (4) to "a credential is never an action target" (e.g. ADMIN <proof> as a connection-level step) rather than merging the admin tier into pairing; PLAN 83.13 keeps the tiers deliberately. (4) Correct the cost figures: the repo paid ~383 src lines at 65.5 for token/bind/URL fix (937 with tests/docs, WirePeer excluded), 44 at 75.1/75.4 and 243 at 76.6 for the passcode retrofit, plus the boot scrub; the alternative is ~60 lines and two tests for the router gate, lights and mirroring fix, not "under 200 lines" including proof mode. (5) Replace "no document states the threat model / invisible by construction" with "REMOTE.md:39-40 discloses that OSC is ungated, but Super Check and the Eye paint it green and ADR-003's TCP/HTTP scoping is easy to misread". (6) "13 days of an ungated OSC path" → 3 days as a bypass of an existing gate (09-15 to 09-18) after 10 days when no transport was gated. (7) "Four places" → three (ShowState.cs:2247, PairingToken.cs:11-12, REMOTE.md:36-37). (8) "The OSC bypass is the tested behaviour" → the bypass is untested (OscAppTests sets no token). (9) The failover consequence is narrow: the Companion module does not follow a takeover, and a standby with the default empty token answers open; the defect is the false sentence, not a lost show. (10) Timing: "day 2" is aspirational under the constraints; round 65.5 done right (router gate, lights, honest sentence) is the fair counterfactual, and the twin-key-before-remote-token ordering at round 34 was correct.

### G6. Keep plaintext transport, LAN trust and the no-admin raw listeners for the portable desk — but as a written decision with its trigger for revisiting, not an omission reconstructed by grep
- Gap: The control link's transport s · id SEC-2 · Kind: keep · Impact (investigator): medium · Confidence: high · When: Round 44 (§62.4, 2026-09-13), when transport encryption was weighed for the twin and deferred with a reason; the same paragraph should have covered the control link and OSC, and become an ADR at round 65.12 when the other fifteen were written.
- What happened: TLS was deferred for the twin link with a sound reason ('a link that must cross a shared network wants TLS with a certificate pinned by the key, which is a round of its own', PLAN §62.4) and never raised for the control link at all. The day-2 choice of raw TcpListener 'no admin rights, no URL ACLs, portable' is right for a USB-stick exe: https for phone browsers on a venue LAN needs a certificate the venue does not have or a self-signed warning on every phone, and the WAN check-in already insists on https outside RFC1918 (RemoteAdmin.cs:550-558) — the correct split. But because the assumption was never named, four credentials added on four days each chose their own transport rules (admin passcode: query string then header then a protocol-line argument; management token: header over https-or-private; twin key: nonce/HMAC, never sent, auto-generated; pairing token: in clear, empty-is-open), and the REMOTE.md Trust note describes 'the trust model of most stage-control protocols' without saying what this protocol leaks. 'Threat model' appears nowhere in 2.1 MB of docs.
- What instead: Keep the transport exactly as it is. Add ADR-016 'Transport is plaintext on the show network' (about twenty lines): the assumption (the control network is the show's own VLAN or a direct cable), what a device on that network can do with and without a token, that OSC is not gated, that the twin key is proof-based and the pairing token should be too, that the settings file holds secrets in clear beside the exe, and the two triggers that reopen the decision (a remote that must cross a guest or shared network; a browser-based remote that needs a secure context, e.g. for WebRTC or clipboard APIs). Put the same three sentences in REMOTE.md's Trust note and in Super Check's amber FIX words. Adopt the rule 'write the threat statement with the first listener': every new port or credential (there were six: TCP, HTTP, OSC, audience, twin, beacon) gets one line in that ADR before it merges.
- Why it matters: A written threat model is the cheapest security artefact in the repository and the one that would have made the round-65 designer notice that the template two days old in Twin.cs was the right one. Its absence cost three external reviews (the maintainer's 13-item critique, the generalisation critique, this retrospective) the work of reconstructing the trust design from grep, left an integrator reading REMOTE.md with a favourable comparison to 'most stage-control protocols' and no statement of what a sniffer learns, and let the same code base ship a proof-based key and a cleartext token side by side without anyone writing down why they differ.
- Evidence: docs/PLAN.md:4527, :6196-6200 (§62.4); docs/REVIEW.md:273, :930 | e43e28b ControlService.cs header comment 'Raw TcpListener on purpose: no admin rights, no URL ACLs, portable' | src/Patterns.Core/Services/RemoteAdmin.cs:550-558 (https outside private ranges) | docs/REMOTE.md:26-40 Trust note; docs/ADR.md headings (15 ADRs, none on transport); grep for 'threat model' across docs and src returns nothing | credential timeline: 8e0949c (09-05, AdminPasscode + ManagementToken), e491a8a/88c8c77 (09-12, twin key), 95c0fc7 (09-13, HMAC proof), 778003d (09-15, pairing token in clear) | src/Patterns.Core/Services/SettingsStore.cs:45, :399; src/Patterns.Core/Services/Secrets.cs:9-26; docs/PLAN.md:15 (USB-stick brief)
- Skeptics:
  - evidence: 1) 'Because the assumption was never named' is too strong. LAN trust for the control wire IS named in passing three times: PLAN:4527 (§48.2, round 31: 'the same LAN trust the control wire and the beacon already assume'), REVIEW.md:273, and ADR-003's Why ('A venue network is shared; a verb from any laptop that found the port must not move the wall'). What is genuinely absent is a statement of what travels in clear, what a sniffer learns, and the triggers for revisiting; reword to 'named only in passing, never as a decision'. Note also that the two written assumptions disagree: §62.4 says the link 'is meant for the show's own VLAN' while ADR-003 says 'a venue network is shared' - which strengthens the case for one written statement. 2) 'docs/ADR.md headings (15 ADRs, none on transport)' should acknowledge ADR-003 (control-network trust: bind + pairing token) and ADR-009 (secrets list) as the nearest existing decisions; ADR-016 would extend them, not fill a void. 3) 'become an ADR at round 65.12 when the other fifteen were written': commit 8c97004 (round 65.12, 2026-09-16) wrote ADR-001..011 only; ADR-012 came at round 69, ADR-013 at 70.4 (c578ee3), ADR-014 at 71 (8794153), ADR-015 at 72.8 (c86ea66). Say 'the other eleven'. 4) Admin passcode transport order 'query string then header then a protocol-line argument' is wrong: at 8e0949c (round 12) the passcode rode a ?pass= query string, a 'passcode\ncommand' POST body AND the 'RESTART <passcode>' / 'UPDATE APPLY <passcode>' wire argument all at once; the X-Patterns-Pass header replaced the query string at 778003d (round 65.5). So: 'query string, POST body and wire argument together at round 12; header replacing the query string at round 65'. 5) 'cost three external reviews (the maintainer's 13-item critique, the generalisation critique, this retrospective)': docs/ROADMAP-ASSESSMENT.md's 13 items contain no trust or security item at all; only docs/GENERALISATION.md (rows 21-22: RECORD ON should require pairing; passcode leaking into action feeds/journal) reconstructed trust behaviour from code. Say 'two reviews'. 6) RemoteAdmin.cs:550-558 allows slightly more than RFC1918: also localhost/::1 and any host ending in '.local'. 7) Add to the proposed ADR-016 content: OSC and the beacon bind IPAddress.Any regardless of ControlConfig.Bind (OscService.cs:110, BeaconService.cs:98), so REMOTE.md's 'Bind to ... the audience network never sees the control ports' does not extend to OSC - a concrete thing the threat statement would have caught. 8) Besides the six named listeners there are also an mDNS socket, a device-discovery UDP socket (Patterns.Devices) and a loopback-only OAuth callback listener; none carries a credential, so the 'six' framing stands if scoped to credential-bearing ports.
  - counterfactual (REFUTED this one): Retitle and downgrade: "Index the existing trust statements in one ADR and fix the one that is wrong" — kind: keep, impact: low, confidence: high. When: round 65.12, as an appendix to ADR-003 rather than a new ADR. What happened (corrected): the plaintext/LAN-trust decision was written seven times (README day 2; REMOTE.md Trust note day 2 and round 65; INSTALLS.md:87-90 and :190 round 12; REVIEW round 34; PLAN §62.4 round 44; ADR-003 and PLAN §83.14 round 65; Super Check's amber row) but never cross-referenced, so a reader of any one file sees one link's rule and not the others'. What instead: add to ADR-003 (a) one paragraph listing the six listeners with a pointer to the sentence that already governs each; (b) one sentence stating that on the control network the pairing token and the X-Patterns-Pass header are readable and replayable by a passive listener, and that the twin key is proof-based because round 44 found the JOIN and SHOW leaking credentials — why the two differ, not that one should copy the other; (c) the two reopen triggers — a remote that must cross a guest or shared network (already stated for the twin in §62.4), and a browser page that needs a secure context; (d) correct REMOTE.md:36 — Control is a TwinSync.LocalSection (Twin.cs:314-318), so the pairing token is not mirrored to the twin, and either the sentence or the section list is wrong; add a test either way. Drop the claims that the assumption was "never named", that "Threat model appears nowhere" implies the content is absent, that three external reviews had to reconstruct the design (the 13-item critique has no trust item; the generalisation critique's two real findings postdate ADR-003/ADR-009), and that "the template two days old in Twin.cs was the right one" for the pairing token (Generic TCP decks and plain-http phone pages cannot compute a proof; round 65 chose the bearer token with the HMAC in view). Drop the proposed rule "write the threat statement with the first listener" — the record shows it was already followed for each listener; the gap is the missing index. Keep "keep the transport exactly as it is" and the observation that Super Check's amber FIX words could add "and a device on that network can read the token off the wire".
  - cost: Impact: medium -> low. Confidence: high -> medium. Corrections to what_happened/why_it_matters: (a) ADR-003 "Explicit control-network trust" and ADR-009 "Secrets are one list" exist and cover roughly half of the proposed ADR-016; the gap is the plaintext sentence, the twin-key/pairing-token asymmetry and a Revisit-when, not an absent trust ADR. (b) The LAN-trust/in-the-clear assumption was named for the control link at round 12 (INSTALLS.md:88-90, mirrored in InstallSection.axaml:116) and for secrets on disk (INSTALLS.md:190), and for OSC (REMOTE.md:38-39); it is scattered, not missing. (c) Neither docs/ROADMAP-ASSESSMENT.md nor docs/GENERALISATION.md mentions transport, TLS or trust — only this retrospective paid a reconstruction cost, while the round-65 roadmap (PLAN §83.14 P1.9) did raise control-network trust and got ADR-003. (d) The revisit triggers are hypothetical today: no phone page uses a secure-context API, and rounds 76-78 field evidence contains no trust item. Preferred form: instead of ADR-016, add ~8 lines and a "Revisit when" to ADR-003 at round 65.12, plus the one-line-per-listener rule; total cost ~10 lines, in line with the 26-36-line marginal ADR cost measured in this repo.

### G8. Keep the small-round cadence, but measure the rebuild in insertions and agent-hours; drop 'fewer rounds' as the target
- Gap: The alternative plan is never · id SUM-2 · Kind: keep · Impact (investigator): medium · Confidence: high · When: From round 40 (e8ee91f, 09-13), when the agent self-adopted per-item round subjects and rounds shrank to minutes
- What happened: The repository shows the round is not a unit of work: 42 of 63 tagged rounds have at most 1.0 h between first and last commit, 24 are single commits, rounds 48-53 closed six rounds in 101 minutes (09-14 00:14-01:55), and rounds 36-38 each landed 2.6-2.9K src lines in one commit. The document nevertheless states its thesis as 'a lower round count', quotes a cadence (9, 11, 7, 8) that the tag table contradicts (13, 11, 6, 8), misplaces rounds 48-52 onto 09-13, and prices retrofits by intra-round commit time ('79 minutes', '3.5 hours'), a measure that reads 0.0 h for a single-commit round. The per-round papers cost only ~298 doc lines a round (11% of insertions), so removing rounds saves hours, not days.
- What instead: Keep closing work in small rounds with papers (it is what let round 78 pin eleven bad takes to the second and ship a 5-commit fix in 2.1 h), but state the rebuilt plan's costs and savings in insertions and agent-hours against the observed pace (22.5-23.8 active hours and ~25K insertions per day on 09-13..09-17), and describe the outcome as 'about one calendar day and 28K insertions of scope', not a round count. Where commit timestamps are used to price a round, say they under-count single-commit rounds.
